ORDINARY ANALYTICAL PROOF SUPPLEMENT
====================================
Companion to main.tex, 27 October 2026.

This is a public source-proof appendix, not a record of acceptances. It contains
actual ordinary derivations, with their original LaTeX labels, followed by the
additional ordinary proofs used by the active route. It is intentionally a
plain UTF-8 source appendix: the formulas can be read as LaTeX; no missing
cross-reference is silently promoted into a proved proposition.

How to read the logical argument
-------------------------------
The main theorem fixes the numerical predicates. The analytical direction is:
unrestricted attained owner -> contact on the entire closed support -> acquired
support and signed moments -> one simultaneous physical budget -> conditional
count identity and complete closed-cell comparison -> all-law exclusion.
Each application fixes its own owner and raw threshold throughout. Weak global
predecessors give q=0 for that same law. N1 and the fixed Bernoulli lower limit
have separate proofs in main.tex. G3 -> G4 -> new one-free C5 starts the finite
chain; the independent positive-ceiling/local branch proves n>=49 without a
sharp1--48 premise. The main text supplies the current13-role assembly, including
the updated low25/low2639/floor40 route and the exact three-loss49 calculation.
The older global assemblies, discarded one-summand premise, historical search
narrative and unused finite-index alternatives are not imported here.

Numerical scope is retained literally in every extracted lemma. An ordinary
proof of an implication does not assert its numerical premise. Conversely, a
certificate/registry/hash does not prove an analytical implication. Source line
numbers locate the original formulas, rather than serve as their justification.

Notation and reference convention
--------------------------------
Main definitions: EX=0, EX²=1, rho=E|X|³, R_n=sqrt(n)Delta_n/rho, C_n=sup_P R_n,
C_E=(3+sqrt10)/(6sqrt(2pi)). H is the raw sum threshold and z=H/sqrt(n).
Within a section, beta=E[X|X|], gamma=EX³, sigma=sqrt10, and eta5=E|X|^5.
The ROOT4 excess parameter eta is distinct from eta5; local symbols are defined
at their original section boundaries. All terminating decimals are rationals.
The source macros mean E/Pp/Prob=expectation/probability, ind/one=indicator,
pos(x)=(x)_+, cR=raw remainder, supp=closed support, phiZ/phiZero=phi(0).
Original labels are preserved without renumbering. A reference to an omitted
historical assembly is only source context; the applicable current assembly is
the one proved in main.tex. Cross-half normalization is proved in main.tex
Appendix Normalization across one half and additionally reproduced at A0 below.

pointwise and compensated identity; A2 direct finite Gaussian far-ray bound;

============================================================================
S1. Sharp moment domain and exact closed-half-line kernel
Original M159--278; use in main: Main support/Fourier reduction; physical ROOT4 kernel
============================================================================

\section{The moment domain and the half-line kernel}
\label{elementary:section}

Let $\mathcal P$ be the centered, unit-variance real laws with finite
$\rho=\E|X|^3$, and put $\gamma=\E X^3$. We use the sharp
third-moment inequality of \cite[Theorem~6]{Shevtsova2014}. Its elementary
factorization is included because the two- and three-summand certificates
use the resulting moment domain.

\begin{theorem}[Sharp third-moment inequality]\label{orig:thm:defect}
Every $P\in\mathcal P$ satisfies
\[
 \rho\ge \frac{\gamma^2+2}{\sqrt{\gamma^2+4}}.
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Set $s=\sqrt{\gamma^2+4}$, $a=(s-\gamma)/2$, $b=(s+\gamma)/2$ and
$c=\gamma(\gamma^2+6)/s^3$. Then $a,b>0$, $ab=1$, and $a+b=s$.
For
\[
 w_\gamma(y)=|y|^3-cy^3-\frac{12y^2}{s^3}
                       +\frac{6\gamma y}{s^3}+\frac4{s^3},
\]
direct multiplication gives, for $y,t\ge0$,
\[
 w_\gamma(y)=\frac{2a^2}{s^3}(y-b)^2\{2+(a+3b)y\},\qquad
 w_\gamma(-t)=\frac{2b^2}{s^3}(t-a)^2\{2+(3a+b)t\}.
\]
Thus $w_\gamma\ge0$. The first three moments and
$c\gamma+8/s^3=(\gamma^2+2)/s$ yield
\[
 0\le\E w_\gamma(X)=\rho-\frac{\gamma^2+2}{s}.
\]
\end{proof}


The Prawitz kernel used throughout is
\[
 K(t)=\frac{1-t}{2}+\frac{\ii}{2}
 \left\{(1-t)\cot(\pi t)+\frac1\pi\right\},\qquad0<t<1,
\]
and
\[
 K(t)={\ii\over2\pi t}+{1\over2}+O(t),\qquad
 \overline{K(t)}=-{\ii\over2\pi t}+{1\over2}+O(t)
 \quad(t\downarrow0).
\]
The corresponding one-sided complex improper integral need not exist.  For
$\kappa\in\{K,\overline K\}$, $0<b\le1$, and
$g(t)=g(0)+O(t)$ with $g(0)\in\mathbb R$, define instead
\[
 \mathfrak I_{\kappa,b}(g):=
 2\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}\operatorname {Re}
       \int_\varepsilon^b\kappa(t)g(t)\dd t,\qquad
 \mathfrak I_\kappa:=\mathfrak I_{\kappa,1}.              \tag{P0}
\]
Thus every later expression of the form
$2\operatorname {Re}\int_0^b\kappa(t)g(t)\dd t$ is understood as the
limit in (P0).

\begin{lemma}[Prawitz--Beurling half-line kernel]\label{orig:lem:BPcardinal}
Define, in the real-part improper sense of \emph{(P0)},
\[
 M(u):={1\over2}+2\Re\int_0^1K(t)e^{2\pi\ii tu}\,\dd t,
 \qquad e_B(u):=M(u)-1_{(-\infty,0]}(u).                 \tag{P1}
\]
Then
\[
 M(u)\ge1_{(-\infty,0]}(u),\qquad
 1-M(-u)\le1_{(-\infty,0)}(u),\qquad e_B(u)\ge0         \tag{P2}
\]
for every $u\in\mathbb R$.  At the integer nodes the identities are exact:
\[
             M(j)=1_{\{j\le0\}},\qquad
             1-M(-j)=1_{\{j<0\}}\quad(j\in\mathbb Z).  \tag{P3}
\]
If $U$ is integrable and $\psi_U(t)=\E e^{2\pi\ii tU}$, then all
expectations below are finite and
\[
 \E M(U)={1\over2}+\mathfrak I_K(\psi_U),\qquad
 \E\{1-M(-U)\}={1\over2}-\mathfrak I_{\overline K}(\psi_U). \tag{P4}
\]
Thus taking expectations in (P2) involves no formal interchange with the
$t^{-1}$ part of the kernel.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The one-sided smoothing inequality of \cite{Prawitz1972}, in the normalization
whose Fourier kernel is the displayed $K$, applied to the point mass at
$u$, gives $1_{(-\infty,0]}(u)\le M(u)$.  Thus the only imported assertion
here is exactly Prawitz's standard half-line inequality; the second
inequality in (P2) follows from the first at $-u$.  We verify the cardinal
and integrability facts used later.

For $j=0$, the real part of $K$ gives $M(0)=1$.  If
$j\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}$, the elementary cosine term and the bare sine
term integrate to zero, and hence
\[
 M(j)={1\over2}-\int_0^1(1-t)\cot(\pi t)\sin(2\pi jt)\,\dd t.       \tag{P5}
\]
The product following $(1-t)$ is invariant under $t\mapsto1-t$.
Moreover, for $m\ge1$,
\[
 \cot(\pi t)\sin(2\pi mt)
 =1+\cos(2\pi mt)+2\sum_{\ell=1}^{m-1}\cos(2\pi\ell t),          \tag{P6}
\]
and the formula for $-m$ is its negative.  Thus the integral in (P5) is
$\tfrac12\operatorname {sgn}j$, proving (P3).

Finally $2\int_0^1\Re K(t)\,\dd t=1/2$, so for each real $u$
\[
 M(u)=1+2\Re\int_0^1K(t)\{e^{2\pi\ii tu}-1\}\,\dd t.             \tag{P7}
\]
This integral is absolutely convergent near zero because
$K(t)=\ii/(2\pi t)+O(1)$ and
$|e^{2\pi\ii tu}-1|\le2\pi t|u|$.  For integrable $U$, the same bound with
$\E|U|$ permits Fubini in (P7).  It gives the first identity in (P4), and
conjugation gives the second.  The same estimate supplies an integrable
affine bound for $M(U)$.
\end{proof}

============================================================================
S2. Unrestricted attainment, contact, whole-threshold support and strip tests
Original M366--949; use in main: Main attainment/contact lemmas, both support rays, same owner
============================================================================

\section{Fixed-index extremizers and the contact interface}
\subsection{Attainment, including the critical value}
\begin{lemma}\label{cut49:global:attainment}\label{cut49:found:orientation}
For every fixed $n\ge1$, $0<C_n\le\sqrt n$, and a law $P\in\cP$ and
threshold $H$ attain $C_n$ as a positive closed discrepancy.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
A negative closed discrepancy reflects into a positive strict discrepancy.
Approach that threshold from below; continuity of $\Phi$ gives the same
supremum using positive closed discrepancies. Since $\rho\ge1$, $C_n\le\sqrt n$; the symmetric binary law at
$H=n$ gives positivity. Choose positive scores $r_j\to C_n$ with
$r_j\ge c>0$. Then $\rho_j\le\sqrt n/c=:M$. Tightness gives a subsequence
$P_j\Rightarrow P$, and we may also assume $\rho_j\to b$. The estimates
\[
 \int_{|x|>A}x^2\,dP_j\le M/A,\qquad
 \int_{|x|>A}|x|\,dP_j\le M/A^2
\]
preserve the first two moments under weak convergence. Lower
semicontinuity gives $\rho(P)\le b$, so $P\in\cP$.
Chebyshev's inequality on a negative threshold, and the Gaussian upper
tail on a positive threshold, give $|H_j|\le n^{3/4}/\sqrt c$.
Pass to $H_j\to H$. Since $n$ is fixed, weak convergence of products
and the continuous sum map give $P_j^{*n}\Rightarrow P^{*n}$.
For every $\varepsilon>0$, eventually $H_j\le H+\varepsilon$;
closed-set Portmanteau followed by $\varepsilon\downarrow0$ gives
\[
 F_{n,P}(H)-\Phi(H/\sqrt n)\ge C_nb/\sqrt n.
\]
Consequently $C_n\ge C_nb/\rho(P)\ge C_n$. Equality proves attainment
and $\rho(P)=b$. Thus third-moment convergence is a conclusion of
extremality, not an unjustified uniform-integrability assumption.
This proof includes $C_n=\CE$; the strict-violation version is
Lemma 4.1 of \cite{HC}.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Contact at every support point}
For $n\ge2$, fix an attaining pair $(P,H)$, and write
\[
 C=C_n,\quad F=F_{n,P}(H),\quad z=H/\sqrt n,\quad
 \rho=\E|X|^3,\quad\gamma=\E X^3,\quad\beta=\E[X|X|].
\]
Here $\beta$ is a signed second moment; it is not the third absolute
moment denoted by $\beta$ in \cite{HC}.

\begin{lemma}[Contact]\label{cut49:global:contact}\label{cut49:found:majorant}
Define
\begin{align}
 \rho I(y)={}&n^{3/2}\{F_{n-1,P}(H-y)-F\}+ny\phi(z)
 +\tfrac12H\phi(z)(y^2-1)\notag\\
 &+\tfrac32C\rho(y^2-1)-C\{|y|^3-\rho-3y\beta\}.
 \label{cut49:found:influence}
\end{align}
Then $I\le0$ on $\R$ and $I=0$ on the full closed support of $P$.
The support is compact. Equivalently, for $\nu=\mathcal L(H-S_{n-1})$,
$\nu([y,\infty))\le\mathfrak q(y)$, with equality on $\supp P$, where
\begin{align}
 n\mathfrak q(y)={}&nF+\tfrac12\{z\phi(z)+C\rho/\sqrt n\}
 -(\sqrt n\phi(z)+3C\beta/\sqrt n)y\notag\\
 &-\tfrac12\{z\phi(z)+3C\rho/\sqrt n\}y^2
 +C|y|^3/\sqrt n.\label{cut49:found:q}
\end{align}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For fixed $y$, contaminate $P$ by
$P_\varepsilon=(1-\varepsilon)P+\varepsilon\delta_y$, holding $H$ fixed.
Its mean and variance are
\[
 m_\varepsilon=\varepsilon y,\qquad
 v_\varepsilon=1+\varepsilon(y^2-1)-\varepsilon^2y^2.
\]
Write $r_\varepsilon=\E_{P_\varepsilon}|X-m_\varepsilon|^3$.
After standardization, the signed score
\[
 \frac{\sqrt n\,v_\varepsilon^{3/2}}{r_\varepsilon}
 \left[F_{n,P_\varepsilon}(H)-
 \Phi\!\left(\frac{H-nm_\varepsilon}{\sqrt{nv_\varepsilon}}\right)\right]
\]
is at most $C$ and equals $C$ at zero. The raw CDF is a polynomial
in $\varepsilon$, whose derivative there is
$n\{F_{n-1,P}(H-y)-F\}$. Dominated differentiation, using the finite
second moment, gives $r'_0=|y|^3-\rho-3\beta y$.
The derivative of the displayed score is precisely $I(y)$, so $I(y)\le0$.
Integration against $P$ gives $\int I\,dP=0$: in particular
$\int F_{n-1,P}(H-y)\,dP(y)=F$.
The closed CDF is upper semicontinuous. Hence $I$ is upper
semicontinuous, and the closed set $\{I=0\}$ has full $P$ measure;
it contains all of $\supp P$. Rearrangement gives
\eqref{cut49:found:q}. Finally,
$\rho I(y)\le-C|y|^3+O(y^2)$ at this fixed pair, since the CDF term is
bounded and $C>0$. Equality on the support excludes both sufficiently
distant rays. No uniform radius is claimed yet.
\end{proof}

The pointwise equality controls even a support point carrying arbitrarily
little mass. This is why weak closeness to a binary law will not be the
last step of the proof.


\section{One support estimate, uniform in the threshold}\label{cut49:support:theorem}
\subsection{The scale correction}
Write $\phi_0=\phi(0)$.
For $n>1$ put $s_n=\sqrt{n/(n-1)}$ and
$e_n=n(s_n-1)-1/2$.
\begin{lemma}\label{cut49:support:scale}
For every integer $n\ge36$, one has $0<e_n<.384/n$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Put $x=1/n$ and $c=.384=48/125$. Direct expansion gives
\[
 (1-x)(1+x/2+cx^2)^2-1
 =x^2\{(2c-3/4)-(c+1/4)x+(c^2-c)x^2-c^2x^3\}.
\]
The bracket is decreasing for $x\ge0$, since $0<c<1$.
At $x=1/36$ its exact rational value is $823/4050000>0$.
Squaring positive quantities gives
$s_n<1+x/2+cx^2$ for $0<x\le1/36$. Similarly
$(1-x)(1+x/2)^2-1=-3x^2/4-x^3/4<0$, which gives the lower bound.
\end{proof}

\subsection{A signed Gaussian envelope}
Define, for every real $H,t$ and $n>1$,
\[
 \mathcal H_n(H,t)=n^{3/2}\{\Phi((H-t)/\sqrt{n-1})-
             \Phi(H/\sqrt n)\}
       +nt\phi(H/\sqrt n)+\tfrac H2(t^2-1)\phi(H/\sqrt n).
\]
\begin{lemma}[Direct support at every index]\label{cut31:support:direct}
Let \(n\ge2\), and let \((P,H)\) be an unrestricted attained
positive closed maximizing pair, with \(C=C_n>0\).
Put \(s=\sqrt{n/(n-1)}\), \(e=n(s-1)-1/2\),
\(A=\sqrt{n-1}\,\Delta_{n-1}(P)/\rho\), and \(d_P=n(A-C)\).
Suppose \((d_P)_+\le q\), \(s\le S\), \(e\le E\),
\(\rho\le r\), \(|\beta|\le b\), and \(0<c\le C\).
Define
\[
 Q(v)=-v^3+\tfrac32rv^2+3bv+rE,\qquad
 L(v)=\tfrac25(v^2/2+1/2+E)+cQ'(v).
\]
If \(R>0\) satisfies
\[
 Q(R),Q'(R),Q''(R),L(R),L'(R)<0,\qquad 1/5-3c<0,
\]
and, for both \(\eta\in\{-1,1\}\),
\[
 \sup_{H\in\R}\mathcal H_n(H,\eta R)+cQ(R)+rSq<0,
\]
then \(\operatorname{supp}P\subset(-R,R)\).
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The contact identity~\eqref{cut49:found:influence}, the active
discrepancy \(C\rho/\sqrt n\), and the predecessor estimate
for this same law imply, at every support point \(t\),
\[
 0\le\mathcal H_n(H,t)
 +C\{-|t|^3+\tfrac32\rho t^2+3\beta t+\rho e\}
 +\rho s d_P.
\]
Here \(n(sA-C)=s d_P+n(s-1)C\); the scale correction has
no omitted term. Also \(A\le C_{n-1}\), hence
\((d_P)_+\le n(C_{n-1}-C)_+\).
Put \(z=H/\sqrt n\), \(y=(H-t)/\sqrt n\).
Exact differentiation gives
\[
 \partial_t\mathcal H_n=n\phi(z)+Ht\phi(z)-ns\phi(sy).
\]
Taylor's theorem and \(\|\phi''\|_\infty=\phi(0)\) give
\( |n\phi(y)-n\phi(z)-Ht\phi(z)|\le\phi(0)t^2/2\).
Moreover \(\partial_s[s\phi(sy)]=-\phi''(sy)\), so
\(|s\phi(sy)-\phi(y)|\le\phi(0)(s-1)\). Therefore
\[
 |\partial_t\mathcal H_n(H,t)|
 \le\phi(0)(t^2/2+1/2+e)
 \qquad(n\ge2,\ H,t\in\R).
\]
The norm identity follows by maximizing
\((x^2-1)e^{-x^2/2}\); its interior extrema occur at
\(0,\pm\sqrt3\).
Since \(Q'''=-6\), its first three gates imply \(Q(v)<0\)
for every \(v\ge R\). At \(t=\eta v\), the bracket multiplying
\(C\) is at most \(Q(v)\), so its multiplier may be replaced
by \(c\). The signed predecessor term is at most \(rSq\).
The contact expression is consequently bounded by
\(\mathcal H_n(H,\eta v)+cQ(v)+rSq\), whose derivative
in \(v\) is at most \(L(v)\). Since
\(L''=2/5-6c<0\) and \(L(R),L'(R)<0\), this envelope strictly
decreases on the entire closed ray. Its strict endpoint upper
bound contradicts contact on both rays, including endpoints,
exterior atoms and exterior accumulation points.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{The whole threshold line.}
No sampled threshold maximum is needed in this lemma.
For \(R>0\), Gaussian differentiation gives
\[
 |\partial_H^2\mathcal H_n(H,R)|\le M,\qquad
 M=\frac{\sqrt n}{4}\left(\frac n{n-1}+1\right)
       +\frac25R+\frac{2|R^2-1|}{5\sqrt n}.
\]
This follows from \(\|\phi'\|_\infty<1/4\),
\(\|\phi''\|_\infty<2/5\), and
\(\|\phi'''\|_\infty\le2/\pi<4/5\).
For a closed threshold cell of midpoint \(m\) and halfwidth \(w\),
Taylor's theorem bounds both signs by
\[
 \pm\mathcal H_n(H,R)
 \le\pm\mathcal H_n(m,R)
       +|\partial_H\mathcal H_n(m,R)|w+Mw^2/2.
\]
If \(H_0\ge5\sqrt n\), \(H_0-R\ge5\sqrt{n-1}\), and
\(\tau\ge\Phi(-5)\), Mills' bound and monotonicity of
\(H\phi(H/\sqrt n)\) give, on both threshold tails,
\[
 |\mathcal H_n(H,R)|\le n\sqrt n\,\tau
 +(nR+H_0|R^2-1|/2)\phi(H_0/\sqrt n),\qquad |H|\ge H_0.
\]
Finally \(\mathcal H_n(H,-R)=-\mathcal H_n(-H,R)\).
Finite rational CDF, density and square-root enclosures on all
closed cells, together with these two tails, therefore certify
the displayed whole-line endpoint bounds.

In particular the positive-budget support certificate at index 27
uses
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 R=13/5,\quad q=813/2000,\quad c=8171/18000,\quad
 r=6413121/5478500,\\
 b=326352124353/10^{12},\quad E=14331929737/10^{12},\quad
 S=1019049330731/10^{12}.
 \end{gathered}
\]
The contrary hypothesis \(C_{27}\ge C_E+2/45\), the finite
cap and the signed-moment inequality supply these bounds.
The finite support certificate in \cite{EffectiveSupplement}
checks every polynomial gate and both strict endpoint inequalities
on 580 adjacent closed threshold cells covering \([-29,29]\),
and on the two tails. Thus this support implication applies
at 27 without extending an estimate proved only for \(n\ge36\).

Write $v=n-1$, $s=\sqrt{n/v}$, $u=|t|$, and
\[
 A=\frac{u^3-3u}{6},\quad B=\frac{u^4-6u^2+3}{24\sqrt v},
 \quad D=\frac{u^5-15u}{120v}.
\]
\begin{lemma}[Fifth-order identity with a uniform finite remainder]
If $u>\sqrt3$, then, with $z=H/\sqrt v$,
\[
 \frac{\mathcal H_n(H,\pm u)}{s^3}
 =\pm A(1-z^2)\phi(z)+B\phi^{(3)}(z)
                  \mp D\phi^{(4)}(z)+R,
\]
where the sign of the remainder need not be fixed and
\[
 |R|\le E^{(5)}_v(u):=
 \frac{51u^6}{14400v^{3/2}}
 +\frac{51(3u^2-2)}{960v^{3/2}}+\frac{u}{8v^2}.
\]
This statement is uniform over every real $H$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Let $F_w(x)=\Phi(x/\sqrt w)$, with primes denoting $x$ derivatives.
The heat identity $\partial_w F_w=F_w''/2$ and ordinary Taylor's theorem
give, with unlabelled derivatives at $(v,H)$,
\begin{align*}
 F_{v+1}&=F_v+F_v''/2+F_v^{(4)}/8+R_0,
 &|R_0|&\le\sup_{w\in[v,v+1]}\|F_w^{(6)}\|_\infty/48,\\
 F_{v+1}'&=F_v'+F_v'''/2+F_v^{(5)}/8+R_1,
 &|R_1|&\le\sup_{w\in[v,v+1]}\|F_w^{(7)}\|_\infty/48,\\
 F_{v+1}''&=F_v''+F_v^{(4)}/2+R_2,
 &|R_2|&\le\sup_{w\in[v,v+1]}\|F_w^{(6)}\|_\infty/8.
\end{align*}
Expand $F_v(H-t)$ through degree five, with remainder at most
$u^6\|F_v^{(6)}\|_\infty/720$. In
\[
 \frac{\mathcal H_n}{n^{3/2}}=
 F_v(H-t)-F_{v+1}(H)+tF_{v+1}'(H)
             -\frac{t^2-1}{2}F_{v+1}''(H),
\]
the first three derivative coefficients cancel. The next three are
\[
 \left(\frac t2-\frac{t^3}6\right)F_v'''
 +\frac{t^4-6t^2+3}{24}F_v^{(4)}
 +\left(\frac t8-\frac{t^5}{120}\right)F_v^{(5)}.
\]
The remaining absolute value is bounded by
\[
 \left\{\frac{u^6}{720}+\frac1{48}+\frac{u^2-1}{16}\right\}
       \sup_{w\in[v,v+1]}\|F_w^{(6)}\|_\infty
 +\frac u{48}\sup_{w\in[v,v+1]}\|F_w^{(7)}\|_\infty.
\]
Fourier inversion of the Gaussian, differentiated under its absolutely
integrable Fourier transform, gives
\[
 \|\phi^{(k)}\|_\infty
 \le\frac1\pi\int_0^\infty y^k e^{-y^2/2}\,dy.
\]
Integration by parts yields
$\|\phi^{(5)}\|_\infty\le8/\pi<2.55$ and
$\|\phi^{(6)}\|_\infty\le15\phi_0<6$.
Since $F_w^{(j)}=w^{-j/2}\phi^{(j-1)}$, the displayed remainder
becomes $s^3E^{(5)}_v(u)$ after multiplication by $n^{3/2}$.
The three retained derivative coefficients give the identity.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[A lower tangent bound for the fourth Gaussian derivative]
For every real $z$,
\[
 \phi^{(4)}(z)\ge\phi_0(3-15z^2/2).
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The same Gaussian Fourier integral gives
$\phi^{(4)}(z)=\pi^{-1}\int_0^\infty
y^4e^{-y^2/2}\cos(yz)\,dy$.
Use the globally valid inequality $\cos w\ge1-w^2/2$ inside
this integral. Its fourth and sixth Gaussian moments are $3\phi_0$
and $15\phi_0$, respectively.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Uniform signed fifth-order envelope]\label{cut49:support:envelope}
Suppose $u>\sqrt3$ and $A,B,D\ge0$. If
$W=A-15D/2>0$, put
\[
 T=A-3D+\frac{9B^2}{4W}.
\]
If $T\ge A/2+2B+3D$, then, for every real $H$ and either sign,
\[
 \mathcal H_n(H,\pm u)\le s^3\{\phi_0 T+E^{(5)}_v(u)\}.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For positive $t=u$ and $|z|\le1$, divide the retained terms by
$\phi_0$ and use the preceding tangent bound to obtain
\[
 A-3D-(A-15D/2)z^2+3B|z|
 \le A-3D+9B^2/(4W).
\]
Here $(1-z^2)\phi(z)\le(1-z^2)\phi_0$ and
$|\phi^{(3)}(z)|\le3|z|\phi_0$ on this interval.
For $|z|\ge1$ the first term is nonpositive, and the Fourier bounds
$\|\phi^{(3)}\|_\infty<2\phi_0$ and
$\|\phi^{(4)}\|_\infty\le3\phi_0$ give $2B+3D$.
For negative $t=-u$, the first term has global upper bound
$2e^{-3/2}A\phi_0<A\phi_0/2$, and the other terms give
$(2B+3D)\phi_0$. The hypothesis on $T$ covers both exterior cases.
The inequality $2e^{-3/2}<1/2$ follows from four positive terms of
the exponential series at $3/2$. Finally add the uniform remainder.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Finite bands and the exact support polynomial}\label{cut49:support:polynomial}
For integers $36\le N\le L$, use the simultaneous endpoint bounds
\[
 B_+=\frac{u^4-6u^2+3}{24\sqrt{N-1}},\quad
 D_+=\frac{u^5-15u}{120(N-1)},\quad
 D_-=\frac{u^5-15u}{120(L-1)}.
\]
For an infinite index range, set $D_-=0$ instead. Put
\[
 W_*=A-15D_+/2,\qquad
 T_*=A-3D_-+\frac{9B_+^2}{4W_*}.
\]
The proof of Lemma~\ref{cut49:support:envelope} applies uniformly on the whole band
if $W_*>0$ and $T_*\ge A/2+2B_++3D_+$. In particular the favorable
term is frozen at the upper index endpoint, and the adverse terms at
the lower one. No monotonicity of a signed coefficient is assumed.

Let $(P,H)$ be an attained positive closed maximizing pair, standardized
to mean zero and variance one. Write $C=C_n\ge C_E$,
$\rho=\mathbb E|X|^3$, $\beta=\mathbb E[X|X|]$, and
$d=n(C_{n-1}-C_n)_+$. The finite contact/predecessor bound is
\[
 \rho I(t)\le\mathcal H_n(H,t)+
 C\{-|t|^3+3\rho t^2/2+3\beta t+\rho e_n\}+\rho s_nd,
\]
with $I=0$ on the entire closed support. Suppose also, for a finite
band, that $C_n-C_E\ge K/n$, where $K>0$. The published finite cap gives
\[
 c=.4095+K/L<C_n,\qquad
 \rho<r=\min\left\{\frac{.3031(.646)}{c-.3031},
                        \frac{.3322(.429)}{c-.3322}\right\}.
\]
For the unrestricted nonnegative case put $K=0$, $c=.4095$.
The sign-moment inequality
$\rho\ge(1+\beta^2)/\sqrt{1-\beta^2}$ supplies $|\beta|<b$ whenever
$(1+b^2)^2>r^2(1-b^2)$ and $0<b<1$. All these statements concern the
same current law. The lower endpoint cannot replace $L$ in $K/L$.

When a preceding resource enclosure supplies sharper actual bounds
$\rho\le r$ and $|\beta|\le b$, these may instead be used in the
same polynomials. Their validity is the only extra premise.

Use rational $\ell^2\le N-1$ and $S^2>N/(N-1)$. For $u=|t|$ define
\[
 Q(u)=-u^3+3ru^2/2+3bu+.384r/N.
\]
First check $Q<0$, before replacing $C$ by its lower bound $c$.
Replace $1/\sqrt{N-1}$ by $1/\ell$ in the adverse remainder and in
the term $2B_+$ of the domination gate. Write $E_*$ for that rational
remainder. Strict negative contact for every $n$ in the band follows
from the polynomial inequality
\[
 \frac25S^3\left\{(A-3D_-)W_*+
              \frac{(u^4-6u^2+3)^2}{256(N-1)}\right\}
 +S^3E_*W_*+\{cQ+rSq\}W_*<0.
\]
The second polynomial gate is $W_*>0$. The third is
\[
 \{A/2-3D_--3D_+-2B_+\}W_*+
         \frac{(u^4-6u^2+3)^2}{256(N-1)}>0.
\]
This is the cleared-denominator version of the domination hypothesis.
The bound on $T_*$ is positive, so the replacement $\phi_0<.4$ and
$s_n<S$ both have their correct direction.

The exact kernel tests these polynomials on all adjacent closed intervals
of length $.05$ from $R$ to $4.5$. Positive Bernstein coefficients prove
their signs on every point of each cell, including endpoints. On the
rays $u\ge4.5$ use the imported finite bound
$|\mathcal H_n(H,t)|\le u^3/15+u/5+1/2$.
The resulting cubic and its first three derivatives are strictly negative
at $4.5$, as is the coefficient $Q$ before replacing $C$ by $c$.
This excludes the whole rays. Thus, if $d\le q$, actual support is in
$(-R,R)$, including exclusion of arbitrarily small exterior atoms and
exterior accumulation points.

\subsection{The support rows used in this proof}
For the all-index local argument, take $K=0$, $R=3.84$, $q=.19$ on
\[
[45,49],\ [50,59],\ [60,89],\ [90,134],\ [135,199],\ [200,\infty).
\]
Only indices at least 47 are used. The last row uses $D_-=0$;
there is no upper index restriction. The exact checker reconstructs
all closed $u$ cells and the two exterior rays. Missing an index band
or the infinite row is an acceptance failure.

The finite base uses five further rows:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
[N,L]&K&R&q\\\hline
{[40,40]}&1.5&3.3&.88\\
{[41,49]}&1.5&2.9&1.5/40\\
{[45,45]}&.7&3.5&.8341\\
{[46,98]}&.7&3.5&.7/45\\
{[46,46]}&.54&3.4&79/450
\end{array}
\]
The second and fourth are used only on $41\le n\le45$ and
$46\le n\le50$, respectively. Retaining their originally certified
upper endpoints makes their inequalities weaker, so restricting the
application introduces no new arithmetic assertion. These are support
implications, not yet exclusions; the resource calculation supplies
the latter.

\subsection{One bounded-threshold series}\label{cut49:support:series}
Put $c_k=(-1)^k/[2^kk!(2k+1)]$, and let $(x)_j$ denote the rising
factorial. Define the following rational polynomials:
\begin{align*}
 P_j(H,t)={}&\sum_{k=0}^{j+1}
 \frac{c_k(k+1/2)_{j+1-k}}{(j+1-k)!}(H-t)^{2k+1}
       -c_{j+1}H^{2j+3}\\
 &+\frac{(-1)^{j+1}tH^{2j+2}}{2^{j+1}(j+1)!}
       +\frac{(-1)^jH^{2j+1}(t^2-1)}{2^{j+1}j!}.
\end{align*}
In particular,
\[
 P_0=(t^3-3t)/6,\qquad
 P_1=\frac{3t-t^3}{4}H^2+\frac{t^4-6t^2+3}{8}H
                  +\frac{-t^5+10t^3-15t}{40}.
\]
Fix $h>0$, $E(x)=(193/71)^{\lceil x\rceil}$, and
\[
 B_r=\frac{7(h+r)}6E((h+r)^2/6)
       +\left(h+r+\frac{h(r^2+1)}8\right)E(h^2/8).
\]
\begin{lemma}\label{cut49:support:finite-series}
For every $n\ge4$, $|H|\le h$ and $|t|\le r$,
\[
 \left|\mathcal H_n/\phi(0)-\sum_{j=0}^5P_j/n^j\right|
       \le4^7B_r/n^6.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Put $z=1/n$. Integration of the Gaussian exponential series gives
$\mathcal H_n/\phi(0)=S(z)/z$, where
\begin{align*}
 S(z)={}&\sum_{k\ge0}c_k\{(H-t)^{2k+1}(1-z)^{-k-1/2}
                                  -H^{2k+1}\}z^k\\
 &+te^{-H^2z/2}+\tfrac12zH(t^2-1)e^{-H^2z/2}.
\end{align*}
Its constant coefficient vanishes, and expansion of the binomial factor
gives $[z^{j+1}]S=P_j$. At $z_0=1/4$, the sum obtained by replacing
all coefficients by their absolute-value majorants is at most $B_r$:
use $(1-z_0)^{-1/2}<7/6$, $1/(2k+1)\le1$, and $e<193/71$.
The latter bound follows, for example, by summing the exponential series
through degree six and bounding the rest by a geometric series with
ratio $1/8$. All products and rearrangements are therefore absolutely
convergent. The discarded terms have degrees at least seven in $S$,
so their sum at $0\le z\le z_0$ is at most $(z/z_0)^7B_r$.
Divide by $z$ to get the result. This proves a finite uniform bound,
including every threshold in the stated interval and every $n\ge4$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{A common contact test for radius and strip refinement}
\label{cut49:support:panels}
Suppose $n\ge47$, $0\le C-C_E\le.7/n$, $\delta_n\le.19$ and
$|X|\le R_0$. A previously acquired row gives
\[
 \rho\le r,\quad 0\le b_l\le\beta\le b_u<1,\quad |H|\le h.
\]
Define $Q(t)=-|t|^3+3\rho t^2/2+3\beta t$ and
$L_P(t)=C_EQ(t)+\phi(0)(t^3-3t)/6$.
For $t>0$, upper polynomials for $L_P(t),Q(t)$ are
\[
 (1/15-.4095)t^3+1.5(.40974)rt^2+(3(.40974)b_u-.3988/2)t,
 \quad -t^3+1.5rt^2+3b_ut.
\]
For $t<0$, write $u=|t|$ and use
\[
 (-.3988/6-.4095)u^3+1.5(.40974)ru^2+(.2-3(.4095)b_l)u,
 \quad -u^3+1.5ru^2-3b_lu.
\]
These coefficient choices are valid because $b_l\ge0$ is checked first.
On any closed cell of one sign, let $L_J$ be an upper bound for the
first polynomial and let $Q_J\ge0$ bound the positive part of the second.
For $j=1,2,3$, take $m_j$ to be the positive part of the largest tensor
Bernstein coefficient of $P_j$ on $[-h,h]\times J$. For $j=4,5$, the
absolute monomial bound
$\sum_{a,b}|[H^at^b]P_j|h^aR_J^b$ suffices, where
$R_J=\max_{t\in J}|t|$. The support contact is then bounded above by
\begin{equation}\label{cut49:support:paneltest}
 L_J+\frac{.7Q_J}{47}
 +.4\left(\sum_{j=1}^5\frac{m_j}{47^j}
                +\frac{4^7B_{R_J}}{47^6}\right)
 +r\left(.19S+\frac{.384(.425)}{47}\right),
 \qquad S>\sqrt{47/46}.
\end{equation}
Indeed substitute the finite series in contact, split $C=C_E+(C-C_E)$,
and use $C<.40974+.7/47<.425$. Every error retained in
\eqref{cut49:support:paneltest} is nonnegative and decreases with $n$.
A strictly negative rational result therefore excludes the entire cell
for every integer $n\ge47$, including atoms and accumulation points.

For a coordinate strip, conditional centering gives
\[
 X=\ell(Y-p),\quad
 \ell=\sqrt{(1-\theta)/(p(1-p))},\quad
 \beta=(1-\theta)(1-2p)+\kappa,\quad |\kappa|\le\theta.
\]
If $p\in[p_l,p_u]\subset(0,1/2)$ and $\theta\le\Theta$, put
$\ell_l\le\sqrt{(1-\Theta)/(p_u(1-p_u))}$.
The forbidden strip $Y\notin(-.55,1.55)$ maps into
\[
 X\le-\ell_l(p_l+.55),\qquad X\ge\ell_l(1.55-p_u).
\]
Apply \eqref{cut49:support:paneltest} between these thresholds and $\pm R_0$.
An interval beyond the known radius is empty; an equal endpoint is a
singleton that is still checked. If only an upper $\beta$ bound is given,
use $b_l=(1-\Theta)(1-2p_u)-\Theta$, after checking its nonnegativity.

The selected consequences used in the resource schedule are:
\[
\begin{array}{l|c|c|r}
\text{input rows}&\text{prior radius}&\text{conclusion}&\text{closed cells}\\\hline
10\text{ central preparation rows}&3.84&|X|<2.5&1080\\
45\text{ central rows}&2.5&-.55<Y<1.55&1431\\
38\text{ left rows}&2.7&-.55<Y<1.55&3073
\end{array}
\]
The respective largest contact bounds are below $-.084$, $-.097$ and
$-.01658$. These implications acquire no row by themselves: each is
used only after its input row is proved by the preceding resource step.
The left radii are applications of the all-threshold support polynomial,
with $(N,L,K,q)=(47,\infty,0,.19)$ and the actual moment caps
\[
 (r,b,R)=(1.390,.430,3),\qquad (1.2563,.3926,2.7).
\]
Thus the same heat proof supplies the initial radius, both left
contractions, and the five finite-base rows. The bounded-threshold
series supplies the central contraction and both coordinate strips.

============================================================================
S3. Characteristic functions, compensated inversion, complete LOW/HIGH and simultaneous resources
Original M950--4042; use in main: Main Fourier/resource reduction and Appendix physical defect; all finite resource ports
============================================================================

\section{Elementary characteristic-function bounds}
\label{afcore:sec:fourier}

Throughout this section, $X$ is real, $\E X=0$, $\E X^2=1$, and
$\rho=\E|X|^3<\infty$. Write
\[
 f(u)=\E e^{iuX},\qquad g(u)=e^{-u^2/2},\qquad \gamma=\E X^3.
\]
No support, extremality, or predecessor assumption is made here.

\subsection{Real and imaginary coordinates}

\begin{lemma}[Anchored coordinates]\label{afcore:lem:anchor}
For $0\le u\le\pi$,
\begin{align}
 \cos u\le\Re f(u)&\le\cos u+\frac{\rho-1}{6}u^3,
 \label{afcore:eq:real-anchor}\\
 |\Im f(u)|^2&\le\frac{(\rho-1)(\rho+5/3)}{36}u^6.
 \label{afcore:eq:imag-anchor}
\end{align}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Put $\operatorname{sinc}v=\int_0^1\cos(tv)\,dt$, including $v=0$.
The elementary bounds $|\sin v|\le|v|$ give
\[
 |\operatorname{sinc}a-\operatorname{sinc}b|
 \le\frac{|a-b|}{2},\qquad
 |\operatorname{sinc}a-\operatorname{sinc}b|
 \le\frac{|a^2-b^2|}{6}.
\]
For the second inequality, use
$|\cos\sqrt A-\cos\sqrt B|\le|A-B|/2$ for $A,B\ge0$,
and integrate in $t$. Evenness handles either sign of $a,b$.

For $r\ge0$, integration from $1$ to $r$ of the first sinc bound gives
\[
 \left|\cos(ur)-\cos u+
       \frac{u\sin u}{2}(r^2-1)\right|
 \le\frac{u^3}{12}(r-1)^2(2r+1).
\]
Indeed the derivative of the expression inside the absolute value is
$u^2r\{\operatorname{sinc}u-\operatorname{sinc}(ur)\}$, and
$\int_{\min(1,r)}^{\max(1,r)}t|t-1|\,dt
=(r-1)^2(2r+1)/6$.
For $R=|X|$, the weight $W(R)=(R-1)^2(2R+1)$ satisfies
$\E W(R)=2(\rho-1)$. Averaging proves the upper real bound, and in fact
the corresponding two-sided anchor error.

For the sharper lower bound, take $0<u\le\pi$ and
$a=\sin u/(2u)\ge0$. The function $v\mapsto\cos v+av^2$ has its
minimum on $[0,\pi]$ at $u$, since sinc decreases there. For $v\ge\pi$,
\[
 \cos v+av^2\ge-1+a\pi^2\ge\cos u+au^2.
\]
Thus this minimum is global on $[0,\infty)$. Substitute $v=uR$ and
average; $\E R^2=1$ cancels the quadratic term. The case $u=0$ is immediate.

Centering and the second sinc bound give
\[
 |\Im f(u)|
 \le\frac{u^3}{6}\E[R|R^2-1|].
\]
Cauchy--Schwarz, with $W$ as above, yields
\[
 \{\E[R|R^2-1|]\}^2
 \le\E W(R)\,
       \E\frac{R^2(R+1)^2}{2R+1}.
\]
There is no singularity at $R=1$: the factorization used here is
$R|R^2-1|=\sqrt{W(R)}R(R+1)/\sqrt{2R+1}$.
The identity
\[
 r^3+\frac{29r^2+1}{18}
 -\frac{2r^2(r+1)^2}{2r+1}
 =\frac{(r-1)^2(4r+1)}{18(2r+1)}\ge0
\]
shows that the second expectation is at most $(\rho+5/3)/2$.
This proves \eqref{afcore:eq:imag-anchor}. Every expectation used is
finite under the third-moment assumption.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[The sine circle]\label{afcore:lem:sine-circle}
Let
\[
 r=\E X_+=\E X_->0,\quad A=\E X_+^2,\quad B=\E X_-^2,
 \qquad \zeta=\frac{2AB}{r}.
\]
Then $A+B=1$ and
\begin{equation}\label{afcore:eq:sine-circle}
 \zeta^2+\frac{36|\Im f(u)|^2}{u^6}\le\rho^2
 \qquad(u\ne0),\qquad
 \zeta\ge b(\rho):=\frac{\sqrt{\rho^2+8}-\rho}{2}.
\end{equation}
In particular, for $0\le u\le\pi$ the coefficient in the imaginary
bound may be replaced by
\[
 |\Im f(u)|^2\le\frac{u^6}{36}
 \min\{(\rho-1)(\rho+5/3),\,\rho^2-b(\rho)^2\}.
\]
Both bounds also hold for $\gamma^2$ after deleting $u^6/36$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Let $\mu_+,\mu_-$ be the restrictions of the law of $|X|$ to the
strictly positive and strictly negative classes, respectively. For
$a,b>0$, let $P_{a,b}$ put masses $b/(a+b),a/(a+b)$ at $a,-b$.
The measure
\[
 d\nu(a,b)=\frac{a+b}{r}\,d\mu_+(a)d\mu_-(b)
\]
has mass $\Pp(X\ne0)$. Mixing $P_{a,b}$ against $\nu$, and retaining
the original atom at zero, recovers the law of $X$.
For a component, write $f_{a,b}(u)=\E_{P_{a,b}}e^{iuX}$ and
\[
 e_{a,b}=\frac{2a^2b^2}{a+b},\qquad
 \rho_{a,b}=\frac{ab(a^2+b^2)}{a+b}.
\]
The squared-argument sinc bound gives
\[
 \left|\frac{6\Im f_{a,b}(u)}{u^3}\right|
 \le\frac{ab|a^2-b^2|}{a+b}.
\]
Consequently the Euclidean norm of
$(e_{a,b},6\Im f_{a,b}(u)/u^3)$ is at most $\rho_{a,b}$.
Integrate this vector inequality. The two integrals on the right and
in the first coordinate are $\rho$ and $2AB/r$, respectively.
The triangle inequality proves the circle. This argument also covers
an atom at zero, whose vector contribution is zero.

Signwise Cauchy inequalities give
\[
 A^2\le r\E X_+^3,\qquad B^2\le r\E X_-^3,\qquad
 \frac{r^2}{A}+\frac{r^2}{B}\le\Pp(X\ne0)\le1.
\]
Hence $\rho+\zeta\ge1/r$, $AB\ge r^2$ and $\zeta\ge2r$.
All quantities are positive, so
$\zeta(\rho+\zeta)\ge2$. Solving this quadratic gives the stated
lower bound. Finally
$6\Im f(u)/u^3\longrightarrow-\gamma$ as $u\to0$ by dominated
convergence, using $|\sin v-v|\le|v|^3/6$. This also proves the assertions
about $\gamma$.
\end{proof}

On a closed moment band $\rho\in[\rho_-,\rho_+]$, any rational $Z>0$
satisfying $Z^2+\rho_+Z\le2$ is a valid uniform lower bound for
$b(\rho)$. Thus no numerical square root is needed to certify this
part of the imaginary enclosure.

\subsection{An explicit scalar certificate and its convex profile}

\begin{lemma}[Scalar cosine bound]\label{afcore:lem:cosine}
For every real $x$,
\begin{equation}\label{afcore:eq:cosine}
 \cos x\le1-\frac{x^2}{2}+\kappa|x|^3,
 \qquad \kappa=\frac{62}{625}.
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
By evenness take $x\ge0$. For $x\ge T:=625/124$ the right side is
at least one. Let $P_{16}(x)=\sum_{j=0}^8(-1)^jx^{2j}/(2j)!$.
The sixteenth derivative of $\cos x-P_{16}(x)$ is $\cos x-1\le0$,
and its first sixteen initial data vanish. Therefore
$\cos x\le P_{16}(x)$ on the whole nonnegative half-line.
It remains to prove positivity on $[0,T]$ of
\[
 Q(x)=\frac{1-x^2/2+\kappa x^3-P_{16}(x)}{x^3}
      =\sum_{j=0}^{13}q_jx^j,
\]
where the removable value at zero is used and the exact coefficient
vector is
\begin{align*}
 (q_0,\ldots,q_6)&=
 \left(\frac{62}{625},-\frac1{24},0,\frac1{720},0,
 -\frac1{40320},0\right),\\
 (q_7,\ldots,q_{13})&=
 \left(\frac1{3628800},0,-\frac1{479001600},0,
 \frac1{87178291200},0,-\frac1{20922789888000}\right).
\end{align*}
Here is a fully specified finite rational certificate. For $0\le r<80$
set $(a_r,b_r)=(r/16,(r+1)/16)$, and set $(a_{80},b_{80})=(5,625/124)$.
For $0\le j,k\le13$ define
\begin{align}
 c_{r,j}&=\sum_{\ell=j}^{13}q_\ell\binom\ell j
               a_r^{\ell-j}(b_r-a_r)^j,\nonumber\\
 B_{r,k}&=\sum_{j=0}^k c_{r,j}\frac{\binom kj}{\binom{13}j}.
 \label{afcore:eq:bernstein-data}
\end{align}
All $81\cdot14$ numbers are thereby specified without external data.
Their exact minimum is
\begin{equation}\label{afcore:eq:bernstein-min}
 \min_{0\le r\le80,\ 0\le k\le13}B_{r,k}
 =B_{63,12}=\frac{2479189}{65585520000}>0.
\end{equation}
For reproducibility, the complete rational verification of this finite
claim is the following calculation; it evaluates only the coefficients
in \eqref{afcore:eq:bernstein-data}.
\begingroup\small
\begin{verbatim}
from fractions import Fraction as F
from math import comb, factorial
q = [F(0) for j in range(14)]
q[0] = F(62, 625)
for j in range(2, 9):
    q[2*j-3] = F((-1)**(j+1), factorial(2*j))
lower = F(2479189, 65585520000)
minimum = None
for r in range(81):
    a, b = ((F(r,16), F(r+1,16)) if r < 80
            else (F(5), F(625,124)))
    c = [sum(q[l]*comb(l,j)*a**(l-j)*(b-a)**j
             for l in range(j,14)) for j in range(14)]
    for k in range(14):
        value = sum(c[j]*F(comb(k,j),comb(13,j))
                    for j in range(k+1))
        assert value >= lower
        minimum = value if minimum is None else min(minimum,value)
assert minimum == lower
\end{verbatim}
\endgroup
The identity
\[
 Q(a_r+(b_r-a_r)t)
 =\sum_{k=0}^{13}B_{r,k}\binom{13}k t^k(1-t)^{13-k}
\]
follows by expanding either side. Its basis is nonnegative and sums
to one for $0\le t\le1$. Equations
\eqref{afcore:eq:bernstein-data}--\eqref{afcore:eq:bernstein-min}
therefore prove positivity on every entire closed cell, not just at
sampled points. The cells cover $[0,T]$, completing the proof.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Compatible profile]\label{afcore:lem:profile}
Put $c_{\cos}=1-\cos4$, $a=129/1250$ and $A=16a/c_{\cos}$. The function
\begin{equation}\label{afcore:eq:profile}
 q(v)=\begin{cases}
  1/2-\kappa v,&0\le v\le4,\\
  A(1-\cos v)/v^2,&4\le v\le2\pi,\\
  0,&v\ge2\pi
 \end{cases}
\end{equation}
is continuous, nonnegative, convex and nonincreasing on $[0,\infty)$,
and $\cos v\le1-v^2q(v)$ there.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The scalar bound at four gives $c_{\cos}\ge16a>0$, hence $0<A\le1$.
The pieces agree at both endpoints. On the curved piece,
\[
 q'(v)=A\frac{v\sin v-2(1-\cos v)}{v^3}\le0,
 \qquad
 q''(v)=\frac{A}{v^4}
       \{6+(v^2-6)\cos v-4v\sin v\}.
\]
Write the brace as $B(v)$. On $[4,3\pi/2]$,
$B'(v)=-2v\cos v+(2-v^2)\sin v\ge0$.
The elementary bounds $25/8<\pi<22/7$ imply
$7\pi/6<4<3\pi/2$, so $B(4)\ge6-10+8=4$.
On $[3\pi/2,2\pi]$ each remaining term in $B$ is nonnegative,
and $B\ge6$. Thus the curved branch is convex, with derivative zero
at $2\pi$. The upward derivative join at four is equivalent to
\[
 258\sin4+119(1-\cos4)\ge0.
\]
Let $S_{15}(x)=\sum_{j=0}^7(-1)^jx^{2j+1}/(2j+1)!$.
The fifteenth derivative of $\sin x-S_{15}(x)$ is $1-\cos x\ge0$,
with vanishing initial data; hence $\sin x\ge S_{15}(x)$ for $x\ge0$.
The exact rational lower bound at the join is
\[
 258S_{15}(4)+119\{1-P_{16}(4)\}
 =\frac{967616288}{638512875}>1.
\]
This proves the required join. The profile majorizes the cosine defect
by the scalar inequality on $[0,4]$, by $A\le1$ on $[4,2\pi]$,
and by $\cos v\le1$ thereafter.

For completeness, the only bounds on $\pi$ just used follow without
a numerical trigonometric evaluation. Set $L(t)=t-t^3/3$ and
$U(t)=L(t)+t^5/5$. Alternating integration gives
$L(t)<\arctan t<U(t)$ for $0<t<1$.
Machin's identity, obtained by tangent addition in $(0,\pi/2)$, yields
\[
 \frac{25}{8}
 <16L(1/5)-4U(1/239)
 <\pi
 <16U(1/5)-4L(1/239)
 <\frac{22}{7}.
\]
The two outer comparisons are rational substitutions. To check the
angle in Machin's identity, $4\arctan(1/5)-\arctan(1/239)$ is positive,
less than $4/5<1<\pi/2$, and has tangent one.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Whole-law modulus]\label{afcore:lem:modulus}
Let $X'$ be an independent copy of $X$, and let
$D\ge\E|X-X'|^3/2$. For $u\ge0$,
\begin{equation}\label{afcore:eq:modulus}
 |f(u)|^2\le1-2u^2q(uD).
\end{equation}
The universal choice $D=\rho+1$ is valid. In particular the two
nontrivial branches are
\[
 |f(u)|^2\le
 \begin{cases}
  1-u^2+2\kappa Du^3,&uD\le4,\\
  1-2A(1-\cos(uD))/D^2,&4\le uD\le2\pi.
 \end{cases}
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Since $\E(X-X')^2=2$, the measure
$(X-X')^2d\Pp/2$ is a probability measure. Its mean of $|X-X'|$ is
$\E|X-X'|^3/2$. The cosine defect bound and Jensen's inequality give
\[
 1-|f(u)|^2
 \ge\E[(u(X-X'))^2q(u|X-X'|)]
 \ge2u^2q\bigl(u\E|X-X'|^3/2\bigr)
 \ge2u^2q(uD).
\]
Finally $|x-y|^3\le(x-y)^2(|x|+|y|)$ and centering imply
$\E|X-X'|^3\le2\rho+2\E|X|\le2(\rho+1)$.
No stronger universal independent-copy bound is being asserted.
\end{proof}

\subsection{A pointwise complex-power enclosure}

\begin{lemma}[Rectangle gate]\label{afcore:lem:rectangle}
Let $n\ge2$ be an integer, $a\ge0$, $0<x_0\le x_1$, and $Y\ge0$.
If
\[
 (n-2)Y\le\frac32x_0,\qquad
 (2n-1)x_0^n\ge(n-1)a,
\]
then throughout $x\in[x_0,x_1]$, $|y|\le Y$,
\[
 |(x+iy)^n-a|
 \le\max\{|(x_0+iY)^n-a|,|(x_1+iY)^n-a|\}.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Put $z=x+iy$, $r=|z|$, and $F(x,y)=|z^n-a|^2$.
Since $|\arg z|\le|y|/x$, the first gate gives
$\Re z^{n-2}\ge0$. Direct differentiation yields
\begin{align*}
 F_{yy}&=2nr^{2n-4}\{r^2+2(n-1)y^2\}
             +2an(n-1)\Re z^{n-2}\ge0,\\
 F_{xx}&=2nr^{2n-4}\{r^2+2(n-1)x^2\}
             -2an(n-1)\Re z^{n-2}.
\end{align*}
Divide the second expression by $2nr^{n-2}>0$. Its positive term is
$r^{n-2}\{(2n-1)x^2+y^2\}\ge(2n-1)x_0^n$, and its negative term
has magnitude at most $(n-1)a$. Thus $F$ is separately convex.
Its maximum on the rectangle is bounded by its four corners; conjugate
corners have equal values. Equality in either gate, $n=2$, and $Y=0$
are included.
\end{proof}

For a frequency $0\le u<\pi/2$ one may take
\[
 x_0=\cos u,\quad x_1=\cos u+(\rho_+-1)u^3/6,\quad
 a=e^{-nu^2/2},
\]
and any valid uniform imaginary upper $Y$. The gates must hold on the
whole integration cell before this enclosure is used there.
Without those gates, an enclosing disk still gives a complete bound.
Indeed, if $M\ge|f(u)|$ and $E\ge|f(u)-g(u)|$, then for every $n\ge1$
\begin{equation}\label{afcore:eq:telescoper}
 |f(u)^n-g(u)^n|
 \le\min\left\{M^n+g(u)^n,
 E\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}M^jg(u)^{n-1-j}\right\}.
\end{equation}
This is the finite factorization of $z^n-w^n$ and remains valid at equal
or zero bases. For a signed real interval, both endpoint squares are
retained when constructing either disk.

\subsection{The smoothing interface}

We use Prawitz's smoothing inequality in the four-term normalization
of \cite[Lemma~4.1, p.~262]{Shevtsova2012}. This is a cited external inequality,
not a consequence of the preceding elementary lemmas. Put
\[
 \mathcal K(t)=(1-t)+i\{(1-t)\cot(\pi t)+1/\pi\},\qquad0<t<1.
\]
For $c>0$, $0<\tau<1$, and every integer $n\ge1$, it gives
\begin{align}
 \Delta_n\le{}&
 \int_0^\tau |\mathcal K(t)|\,
             |f(ct)^n-g(ct)^n|\,dt
 +\int_\tau^1|\mathcal K(t)|\,|f(ct)|^n\,dt\nonumber\\
 &+\int_0^\tau\left|\mathcal K(t)-\frac{i}{\pi t}\right|
                    g(ct)^n\,dt
 +\frac1\pi\int_\tau^\infty\frac{g(ct)^n}{t}\,dt.
 \label{afcore:eq:prawitz}
\end{align}
Here $\Delta_n$ is the Kolmogorov distance for $S_n/\sqrt n$.
All four terms are nonnegative. The last is
$E_1(nc^2\tau^2/2)/(2\pi)$, where
$E_1(x)=\int_x^\infty e^{-v}\,dv/v\le e^{-x}/x$ for $x>0$.
Normal continuity equates the suprema defined with strict and closed
CDFs, so atoms introduce no additional restriction in this application.

On a moment band, multiply a complete upper bound for the right side
by $\sqrt n/\rho_-$, not by $\sqrt n/\rho_+$.
A finite partition yields an integral upper bound by summing width
times a valid whole-cell majorant. If a fixed $C^2$ majorant $F$ is
used on a cell of width $w$ and midpoint $m$, Taylor integration gives
\[
 \int F\le wF(m)+\frac{w^3}{24}\max\{0,\sup F''\}.
\]
Taking a minimum with a separately valid Darboux upper is legitimate.
Selecting a favorable branch at the midpoint without proving it on
the entire cell is not. Endpoint pieces and the infinite normal tail
remain separate obligations in every finite certificate.

\section{Contact, compensated inversion, and the conserved identity}
\label{afcore:sec:conserved}

We now state precisely the contact input needed for the identity.
Fix an integer $n\ge2$ and a standardized common iid marginal $P$.
Write $S_j=X_1+\cdots+X_j$, $s=\sqrt n$, $z=H/s$,
\[
 F=\Pp(S_n\le H),\quad
 C=\frac{s}{\rho}\{F-\Phi(z)\}>0,\quad
 \beta=\E[X|X|],\quad \gamma=\E X^3,\quad \varphi=\phi(z).
\]
Suppose that the fixed-index contact argument has supplied
\begin{equation}\label{afcore:eq:contact-input}
 h(y):=\Pp(S_{n-1}\le H-y)\le Q_H(y),
 \qquad h(y)=Q_H(y)\quad(y\in\operatorname{supp}P),
\end{equation}
where
\begin{align}
 nQ_H(y)={}&nF+\frac{H\varphi+C\rho}{2s}
 -\left(s\varphi+\frac{3C\beta}{s}\right)y
 -\frac{H\varphi+3C\rho}{2s}y^2+\frac C s|y|^3.
 \label{afcore:eq:contact-polynomial}
\end{align}
These explicit inequalities, rather than an unnamed structural
assumption, are the hypothesis below. At an unrestricted maximizing
positive closed-CDF witness they follow from the earlier contamination
argument.

Because $C>0$, the polynomial grows to $+\infty$ on either support ray.
Equality in \eqref{afcore:eq:contact-input}, with $0\le h\le1$,
therefore gives $|X|\le R$ for some finite $R$.
Only after this observation do we introduce
$M_4=\E X^4$ and $M_5=\E|X|^5$.

\subsection{The gap quantities and their signs}

Set $\sigma=\sqrt{10}$ and
$\tau(t)=\E[X\ind_{\{X\ge t\}}]$. Centering gives
$\tau(t)=-\E[X\ind_{\{X<t\}}]\ge0$ for $t<0$; its nonnegativity
for $t\ge0$ is immediate. Signwise Fubini gives
\[
 \int_{\mathbb R}\tau(t)\,dt=1,\qquad
 2\int_{\mathbb R}|t|\tau(t)\,dt=\rho.
\]
For each bounded complementary interval $(a,b)$ of the closed support,
$\tau$ is constant on its interior; denote that value by $\tau_{a,b}$.
Let $\nu$ be the law of $H-S_{n-1}$, and define
\begin{align}
 J&=\sum_{(a,b)}\tau_{a,b}(b-a)^2,\nonumber\\
 J_1&=\sum_{(a,b)}\tau_{a,b}\int_a^b(t-a)t\,dt,\qquad
 J_2=\sum_{(a,b)}\tau_{a,b}\int_a^b(t-a)t|t|\,dt,\nonumber\\
 L&=\sum_{(a,b)}\tau_{a,b}\int_{[a,b)}(t-a)\,d\nu(t),\qquad
 D=(3+\sigma)\rho-\gamma-3J.
 \label{afcore:eq:gap-definitions}
\end{align}
The half-open interval in $L$ is part of its definition.
There are at most countably many gaps. Their lengths are at most $2R$,
so
\[
 J\le2R,\quad |J_1|\le RJ/2,\quad |J_2|\le R^2J/2,
 \quad0\le L\le2R.
\]
For the first inequality, use $(b-a)^2\le2R(b-a)$ and $\int\tau=1$.
For $L$, use $\tau\le\E|X|\le1$ and disjointness of the half-open gaps.
Thus all the displayed sums are absolutely convergent.

\begin{lemma}[Nonnegative physical defect]\label{afcore:lem:physical-defect}
For the quantities in \eqref{afcore:eq:gap-definitions}, $D\ge0$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
If zero belongs to the support, each gap lies on one side of zero.
The inequality $(b-a)^2\le2\int_a^b|t|\,dt$ then gives $J\le\rho$,
and $D\ge\sigma\rho-\gamma\ge0$.

Otherwise there is a central gap $(-A,B)$ with $A,B>0$. Put
\[
 m=\E X_+=\E X_-,\quad
 T_+=\E X_+^3-mB^2,\quad T_-=\E X_-^3-mA^2,
 \quad J_o=J-m(A+B)^2.
\]
Both $T_+,T_-$ are nonnegative. For a positive outer gap $(a,b)$,
\[
 (b-a)^2\le2\int_a^b(t-B)\,dt.
\]
Tonelli, followed by integration up to $X_+$, gives
\[
 J_o^+\le T_+-2B\{\E X_+^2-mB\}\le T_+.
\]
Reflection gives $J_o^-\le T_-$. Direct expansion, using
$\sqrt{\sigma+1}\sqrt{\sigma-1}=3$, yields
\[
 D=m\{\sqrt{\sigma+1}A-\sqrt{\sigma-1}B\}^2
   +(\sigma-1)T_++(\sigma+1)T_-
   +3(T_++T_--J_o)\ge0.
\]
\end{proof}

\subsection{Integrating the same slack in two ways}

Put $\mathcal A=\int\tau(y)\{Q_H(y)-h(y)\}\,dy\ge0$.
On the support the slack vanishes; outside the convex hull $\tau=0$.
On a gap, contact at its endpoints gives
\[
 \nu([a,b))=h(a)-h(b)=Q_H(a)-Q_H(b)
                         =\int_a^b -Q_H'(t)\,dt.
\]
Bounded Fubini consequently gives
\[
 \int_a^b(Q_H-h)
 =\int_a^b(t-a)(-Q_H'(t))\,dt
       -\int_{[a,b)}(t-a)\,d\nu(t).
\]
In particular a mass at the upper endpoint is not counted as interior.
Substituting \eqref{afcore:eq:contact-polynomial} proves
\begin{equation}\label{afcore:eq:first-slack}
 \mathcal A=\frac{\varphi J}{2s}+\frac{G}{s^3}-L,
 \quad
 G=\frac32C\beta J+(H\varphi+3C\rho)J_1-3CJ_2.
\end{equation}

The density $\tau$ is a probability density. If $X^*$ has that density,
signwise Fubini proves, for every $C^1$ test function on the compact range,
\[
 \E g'(X^*)=\E[Xg(X)],\qquad
 \E X^*=\gamma/2,\quad \E(X^*)^2=M_4/3,
 \quad\E|X^*|^3=M_5/4.
\]
Take $X^*$ independent of $S_{n-1}$. Applying the identity separately to
the actual iid summands shows that $Z^*=(S_{n-1}+X^*)/s$ has density
$\tau_Z(t)=\E[Z\ind_{\{Z\ge t\}}]$, where $Z=S_n/s$.
Its CDF at $z$ is
\[
 T=\E[(Z^2-zZ)\ind_{\{Z\le z\}}].
\]
For example, this formula follows by signwise integration of $\tau_Z$;
it is unaffected by endpoint atoms. Since $\E h(X^*)=T$, integration
of the contact polynomial against $\tau$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{afcore:eq:second-slack}
 \mathcal A=F-\frac{\gamma\varphi}{2s}
 +\frac{H\varphi(3-M_4)/6+CK_q}{s^3}-T,
 \quad K_q=\frac{M_5}{4}-\frac{\rho M_4}{2}
                  -\frac{3\beta\gamma}{2}+\frac\rho2.
\end{equation}

There is one further contact equality. Multiplication by $X$ and
exchangeability give $n\E[Xh(X)]=\E[S_n\ind_{\{S_n\le H\}}]$.
Thus, if
\[
 M=\E[Z\ind_{\{Z\le z\}}],\qquad
 K_1=\E[X|X|^3]-3\beta-\frac32\rho\gamma,
\]
then
\begin{equation}\label{afcore:eq:truncated-first}
 M+\varphi=-\frac{\gamma z\varphi}{2s}+\frac{CK_1}{n}.
\end{equation}
All moments here are legitimate because support was established first.

\subsection{Compensated inversion over the entire half-line}

\begin{lemma}[Quadratic hinge inversion]\label{afcore:lem:inversion}
Let $\mu$ be a finite signed measure on $\mathbb R$ with zero mass,
zero first moment, and finite absolute second moment. With
$\widehat\mu(t)=\int e^{itx}\,d\mu(x)$, for every real $H$,
\begin{equation}\label{afcore:eq:inversion}
 \int(x-H)|x-H|\,d\mu(x)
 =-\frac4\pi\int_0^\infty
        \frac{\Im(e^{-itH}\widehat\mu(t))}{t^3}\,dt.
\end{equation}
The right side is absolutely convergent.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Scaling and integration by parts give
\[
 \int_0^\infty\frac{ty-\sin(ty)}{t^3}\,dt
 =\frac\pi4 y|y|.
\]
For $y\ge0$ the integrand is nonnegative, and for $y<0$ it has the
opposite sign. Its absolute integral is therefore $\pi y^2/4$.
To check the constant, one integration by parts reduces it to half of
$\int_0^\infty(1-\cos t)t^{-2}\,dt$. Since
$t^{-2}=\int_0^\infty a e^{-at}\,da$, nonnegative Tonelli and elementary
Laplace integration give
\[
 \int_0^\infty\frac{1-\cos t}{t^2}\,dt
 =\int_0^\infty a\left\{\frac1a-\frac a{a^2+1}\right\}\,da
 =\int_0^\infty\frac{da}{a^2+1}=\frac\pi2.
\]
Absolute Fubini applies to the compensated integrand against $|\mu|$.
The linear term integrates to zero, since both the mass and first moment
vanish. This proves the formula and its absolute convergence. No CDF
inversion or averaging of a jump is used.
\end{proof}

Let $N$ be standard normal and set
\[
 q_z(x)=(x-z)|x-z|,\qquad
 \varepsilon=\E q_z(Z)-\E q_z(N)-\frac{2\gamma\varphi}{3s}.
\]
Apply Lemma~\ref{afcore:lem:inversion} to the law of $S_n$ minus the
scale-$s$ pushforward of the signed density
\[
 \phi(x)\left\{1+\frac{\gamma}{6s}(x^3-3x)\right\}.
\]
That density has mass one and first moment zero. Gaussian integration
by parts gives its pushed-forward transform
$e^{-nt^2/2}(1-in\gamma t^3/6)$.
The hinge correction is $2\gamma\varphi/(3s)$, because
$(x^3-3x)\phi(x)=-\phi'''(x)$ and
$-\int q_z\phi'''=4\phi(z)$, with the third derivative of $q_z$
understood distributionally as $4\delta_z$.
Since $q_H(sx)=nq_z(x)$, the exact normalization is
\begin{equation}\label{afcore:eq:epsilon}
 \varepsilon=-\frac4{\pi n}\int_0^\infty
        \frac{\Im(e^{-itH}V_n(t))}{t^3}\,dt,
 \quad V_n(t)=f(t)^n-e^{-nt^2/2}(1-in\gamma t^3/6).
\end{equation}
In particular the infinite high-frequency part has not been discarded.

\subsection{The identity and the error ledger}

\begin{proposition}[Conserved identity]\label{afcore:prop:conserved}
Under the explicit contact hypotheses
\eqref{afcore:eq:contact-input}--\eqref{afcore:eq:contact-polynomial},
put $e=C-\CE$, $w=H^2/(2n)$, and
$U=J_1+(M_4-3)/6$. Then
\begin{align}
 ne\rho+n\CE\rho(1-e^{-w})
  +\frac{n\phi(0)e^{-w}}6D+n^{3/2}L
 ={}&C\{3\beta J/2+3\rho J_1-3J_2-K_q\}\nonumber\\
 &+H(CK_1+\varphi U)
   -H^2(C\rho+\gamma\varphi/2)+E_n,
 \label{afcore:eq:conserved}\\
 E_n={}&-n^{3/2}\varepsilon/2.\nonumber
\end{align}
If $C\ge\CE$, every term on the left is nonnegative.
For any split $b>0$, valid complete bounds
\[
 I_{\rm low}\ge\int_0^b
       \frac{|\Im(e^{-itH}V_n(t))|}{t^3}\,dt,\qquad
 I_{\rm high}\ge\int_b^\infty\frac{|V_n(t)|}{t^3}\,dt
\]
give
\begin{equation}\label{afcore:eq:error-ledger}
 |E_n|\le\frac{2\sqrt n}{\pi}(I_{\rm low}+I_{\rm high}).
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The pointwise identity
$q_z(x)=(x-z)^2-2(x-z)^2\ind_{\{x\le z\}}$ gives
\[
 T-\Phi(z)=-\tfrac12\{\E q_z(Z)-\E q_z(N)\}
             +z(M+\varphi)-z^2(F-\Phi(z)).
\]
Here $\E[N\ind_{\{N\le z\}}]=-\varphi$ and the normal counterpart
of $T$ is $\Phi(z)$, by one integration by parts.
Use \eqref{afcore:eq:truncated-first}, the definition of $\varepsilon$,
and $F-\Phi(z)=C\rho/s$ in \eqref{afcore:eq:second-slack}.
If
\[
 P_0=H^2(C\rho+\gamma\varphi/2)
    -H\{CK_1+\varphi(M_4-3)/6\}+CK_q,
\]
the result is
\[
 s\mathcal A=C\rho-\gamma\varphi/6+P_0/n+s\varepsilon/2.
\]
Comparison with \eqref{afcore:eq:first-slack} yields
\[
 C\rho+sL=\frac{\varphi}{6}(\gamma+3J)
                     +\frac{G-P_0}{n}-s\varepsilon/2.
\]
Finally substitute
$\gamma+3J=(3+\sqrt{10})\rho-D$,
$\CE=(3+\sqrt{10})\phi(0)/6$, and
$\varphi=\phi(0)e^{-w}$, and multiply by $n$.
This proves \eqref{afcore:eq:conserved}. Nonnegativity follows from
Lemma~\ref{afcore:lem:physical-defect}, $L\ge0$, and $e\ge0$.
Equation~\eqref{afcore:eq:epsilon} gives the ledger directly.
\end{proof}

The proposition is a fixed-index statement for every $n\ge2$.
It uses neither a limiting expansion nor an eventual theorem.
Quantitative LOW/HIGH estimates, finite resource induction and count
coverage remain distinct obligations; the identity itself does not
establish any numerical support radius or parameter exclusion.

% Mathematical resource chapter, flattened from the supplied continuous proofs.
% No preamble, external inputs, or finite-index success assertions.
% All project labels introduced here use rescore:.
\section{Complete Fourier remainders and finite resource enclosure}
\label{rescore:sec:setup}
Use the contact normalization and the gap quantities of
Section~\ref{afcore:sec:conserved}. Thus $C$ is the actual positive
closed-CDF score of one unrestricted attaining law, $H$ is its raw
sum threshold, $\rho=\E|X|^3$, $\gamma=\E X^3$,
$\beta=\E[X|X|]$, and $D,J,J_1,J_2,L$ have the definitions in
\eqref{afcore:eq:gap-definitions}. Let
$\mu_j=\E X^j$ for even $j$, $\eta_5=\E|X|^5$, and
$\varphi=\phi(H/\sqrt n)$. Support $[-R_-,R_+]$ is acquired before
these higher moments are invoked; write $R=\max(R_-,R_+)$.
In applications below $1\le R\le6$.
Define the exact raw remainder, without subtracting a score charge, by
\begin{equation}\label{rescore:re-raw}
 \mathcal R=C\{3\beta J/2+3\rho J_1-3J_2-K_q\}
 +H(CK_1+\varphi U)-H^2(C\rho+\gamma\varphi/2)+E_n,
 \qquad U=J_1+(\mu_4-3)/6 .
\end{equation}
The definitions of $K_q,K_1,E_n$ and the exact identity for
$\mathcal R$ are in Section~\ref{afcore:sec:conserved}.
The elementary constant enclosures used here are
\[
 997/2500<\phi(0)<2/5,\qquad
 .4095<\CE<.40974,\qquad 333/106<\pi<22/7.
\]
They follow by positive rational squaring from Machin's identity. Put
\[
 A_m(t)=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\frac{(-1)^jt^{2j+1}}{2j+1}.
\]
Use
\begin{align*}
 A_{32}(1/5)&<\arctan(1/5)<A_{32}(1/5)+5^{-65}/65,\\
 A_{10}(1/239)&<\arctan(1/239)<A_{10}(1/239)+239^{-21}/21.
\end{align*}
The scalar square-root comparisons are between positive quantities.

A finite-index profile means
\begin{equation}\label{rescore:u-profile}
 N\le n\le L,\quad n(C-\CE)\ge K\ge0,\quad
 C\in[c_0,c_1],\quad n(C_{n-1}-C)_+\le q_*,
 \qquad c_0=.4095+K/L .
\end{equation}
A band-uniform bound is used only under the explicit monotonicity
gates proved below. Otherwise every formula is evaluated at the
actual integer $n$. In particular, no monotonicity of $C_n$ is assumed.
The quantitative support implication is a separate contact argument,
not an output of the Fourier integration.

\subsection{One complete low-frequency transfer}
Put $f(t)=\E e^{itX}$, $h(t)=e^{-t^2/2-i\gamma t^3/6}$,
$e_n(t)=e^{-nt^2/2}(1-in\gamma t^3/6)$, and
\[
 I_0(H)=\int_0^{1/2}|\Im(e^{-itH}(f^n-e_n))|\frac{dt}{t^3},\qquad
 I_\infty=\int_{1/2}^\infty|f^n-e_n|\frac{dt}{t^3}.
\]
The inversion bound~\eqref{afcore:eq:error-ledger} charges
$2\sqrt n(I_0+I_\infty)/\pi$; both integrals below are complete.

\begin{lemma}[Signed Gaussian transfer]\label{rescore:fourier-early-low}
\label{rescore:fourier-paired-low}\label{rescore:entryleap-fourier-transfer}
Suppose $n\ge N>2$, $\mu_4\le M\le11.1$, $|\gamma|\le g\le1.85$,
$\mu_6\le M_6$, and the actual coefficients
\[
 a=(3-\mu_4)/24,\quad z=(10\gamma-\E X^5)/120,\quad c=a\gamma/6
\]
satisfy $|a|\le a_*$, $|z|\le Z$. Put
\begin{equation}\label{rescore:fourier-taylor}
 E_6=M_6/720+g^2/72+(1/2+g^2/144)^3/5,\quad
 B_6=a_*+Z/2+E_6/4.
\end{equation}
Let $a_0=(N-1)/(2N)$,
$0<b_0\le(45-M)(1-2/N)/96$, and
$\lambda_N=\sqrt{\pi/2}(N/(N-1))^{3/2}$.
Then, for every real $H$,
\begin{equation}\label{rescore:fourier-low-form}
 I_0(H)\le(L_0+L_H|H|)/\sqrt n+L_2/n,
 \qquad L_2=E_6/(2a_0^2)+B_6^2/(2b_0^3).
\end{equation}
Two admissible choices, each used as a complete triple, are as follows.
The triangle choice is
\begin{equation}\label{rescore:fourier-early}
 L_0=c_0Z+c_1a_*g+251g^3/17280,\quad
 L_H=c_0a_*+251g^2/4800,
\end{equation}
where $c_0\ge\lambda_N$, $c_1\ge\lambda_N/2$.
For the cancellation choice, take any rectangle containing the actual
$(z,c)$, $I_u\ge\sqrt{\pi/2}$ and $F_N\ge(N/(N-1))^{3/2}$. Define
\begin{gather*}
 U(z,c)=\begin{cases}
 I_u(|z|+3|c|),&zc\le0,\\
 I_u\bigl||z|-3|c|\bigr|+6\sqrt{|zc|}e^{-|z|/(2|c|)},&zc>0,
 \end{cases}\\
 J_u=\max_{\rm corners}U(z,c).
\end{gather*}
Then
\begin{align}\label{rescore:fourier-paired}
 L_0&=F_NJ_u+\frac{F_NI_u}{N-1}
 (35a_*g^3/48+35Zg^2/24)+251g^3/17280,\notag\\
 L_H&=F_NI_u\{a_*+5Zg/[2(N-1)]\}+251g^2/4800.
\end{align}
Neither the transfer nor its cancellation choice assumes signs for
$a,z,c$ or $\gamma$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Taylor with integral remainder gives
$f-h=-at^4-izt^5+r_6$, $|r_6|\le E_6t^6$ for $0\le t\le1/2$.
Indeed for $w=-t^2/2-i\gamma t^3/6$,
$|w|\le t^2(1/2+g^2/144)<1/6$; the terms through degree five in
$1+w+w^2/2$ are exact, its degree-six term costs $g^2t^6/72$, and
the remaining exponential series costs $|w|^3e^{|w|}/6\le|w|^3/5$.
Add the true sixth-moment remainder.

Four integrations of $1-\cos u\ge0$ give
$\cos u\le1-u^2/2+u^4/24$. Symmetrization, using
$\E(X-X')^4=2\mu_4+6$, therefore gives
$|f(t)|,|h(t)|\le e^{-(45-M)t^2/96}$ on $[0,1/2]$.
Every point on their joining segment has the same bound, so polynomial
Taylor expansion yields
\[
 f^n-h^n=n(f-h)h^{n-1}+R_2,\qquad
 |R_2|\le\tfrac12n^2B_6^2t^8e^{-b_0nt^2}.
\]
The $r_6$ and $R_2$ integrals are at most
\begin{equation}\label{rescore:fourier-common-error}
 \frac1n\{E_6/(2a_0^2)+B_6^2/(2b_0^3)\}=L_2/n.
\end{equation}
For the remaining comparator put $u=n\gamma t^3/6$. Its imaginary part
is bounded by $e^{-nt^2/2}(|u|^3/6+|H|tu^2/2)$, so its divided integral
is at most
\begin{equation}\label{rescore:fourier-comparator-low}
 n^{-1/2}(251g^3/17280+251g^2|H|/4800).
\end{equation}
Here the Gaussian moments follow from integration by parts,
$\int_0^\infty x^{2j}e^{-x^2/2}dx=(2j-1)!!\sqrt{\pi/2}$,
and $\sqrt{2\pi}<251/100$.

It remains to price the leading quartic and fifth terms. Set $b=n-1$,
$v=b\gamma t^3/6$. The triangle estimate uses
$|\sin(Ht+v)|\le|H|t+bg t^3/6$, $|\cos|\le1$; the Gaussian
moments of orders two and four give respectively $\lambda_N$ and
$\lambda_N/2$. This proves~\eqref{rescore:fourier-early}.
For cancellation, after division by $t^3$, rotation by $Ht$ bounds the
leading imaginary part by
\[
 |zt^2-atv|+a_*t|\sin v-v|+Zt^2|\cos v-1|
       +|H|t\{a_*t+Zt^2|\sin v|\}.
\]
Multiply by $ne^{-bt^2/2}$ and use the global sine/cosine remainders.
The first integral equals $nJ(z,c)/b^{3/2}$, where
$J(z,c)=\int_0^\infty e^{-x^2/2}|zx^2-cx^4|dx$.
This norm is convex and $J(-z,-c)=J(z,c)$, so its rectangle maximum
is at a corner. For $zc\le0$ the integral is
$\sqrt{\pi/2}(|z|+3|c|)$. For $z,c>0$, split at $r=\sqrt{z/c}$;
integration by parts gives
\[
 J=\sqrt{\pi/2}(z-3c)+6cr e^{-r^2/2}
       +2(3c-z)\int_r^\infty e^{-x^2/2}dx.
\]
Discard the last term when nonpositive, and otherwise bound its integral
by $\sqrt{\pi/2}$. This proves $J\le U$; simultaneous sign reflection
covers the remaining quadrant. The other four phase terms use moments
of orders two, six, eight and ten and give the coefficients
$1,5/2,35/24,35/48$ in~\eqref{rescore:fourier-paired}. Finally $n/(n-1)$,
$1/(n-1)$, $a_0^{-1}$ and the allowable damping inverses decrease with
$n$, proving the all-index bounds at $N$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Retaining the signed Gaussian tail]\label{rescore:entry189-mills}
For $r>0$, put
\[
 \mathcal M(r)=e^{r^2/2}\int_r^\infty e^{-t^2/2}\,dt,
 \qquad
 \mathcal M_k(r)=\cfrac1{r+\cfrac1{r+\cfrac2{\ddots+\cfrac{k}{r}}}}
 \quad(k\ge1),\qquad \mathcal M_0(r)=1/r.
\]
Odd truncations are lower bounds for $\mathcal M$; even truncations
are upper bounds. For $z,c>0$, set $x=z/c$ and
\[
 d_{k+1}=1,\qquad d_j=1+\frac j{xd_{j+1}}\quad(j=k,\ldots,1),
 \qquad m_k(x)=d_1^{-1}.
\]
Then the cancellation norm in Lemma~\ref{rescore:entryleap-fourier-transfer}
satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{rescore:entry189-mills-bound}
 J(z,c)\le \sqrt{\pi/2}(z-3c)+\sqrt{zc}\,e^{-x/2}
 \left(6+\frac{2(3-x)}x m_k(x)\right),
\end{equation}
where $k=11$ if $x\ge3$ and $k=12$ if $x<3$.
The factor in parentheses is positive. One may take the minimum with
the previous bound $U(z,c)$ at each corner of the coefficient rectangle.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The positive integrals
$I_j(r)=\int_0^\infty t^j e^{-rt-t^2/2}\,dt$ satisfy
$I_1=1-rI_0$ and $I_{j+1}=jI_{j-1}-rI_j$ by integration by parts.
Thus, writing $q_j=I_j/I_{j-1}>0$,
\[
 I_0=\frac1{r+q_1},\qquad q_j=\frac j{r+q_{j+1}}.
\]
Replacing the final positive $q_{k+1}$ by zero reverses the inequality
at each substitution. This proves the alternating finite bounds, since
translation gives $I_0=\mathcal M(r)$. No convergence of an infinite
continued fraction is required. The displayed rational recursion gives
$m_k(x)=r\mathcal M_k(r)$ when $r=\sqrt x$.

Splitting the norm at $r=\sqrt{z/c}$, as in the preceding proof, gives
the exact identity
\[
 J(z,c)=\sqrt{\pi/2}(z-3c)+\sqrt{zc}\,e^{-x/2}
 \left(6+\frac{2(3-x)}x r\mathcal M(r)\right).
\]
The multiplier of $\mathcal M$ is nonpositive when $x\ge3$ and
positive when $x<3$. Substitute the corresponding finite lower or
upper bound. Positivity of the remaining factor is immediate for
$x<3$; for $x\ge3$, use $0<m_{11}(x)<1$ to bound it below by
$6-2(x-3)/x>4$. Finally the exact norm $J$, rather than either
upper-bound formula, is convex, so its containing rectangle is still
exhausted by its four corners.
\end{proof}

At every positive coefficient corner use the displayed finite Mills bound.
A negative Gaussian coefficient uses a lower enclosure for
$\sqrt{\pi/2}$; a nonnegative coefficient uses an upper one. The remaining
factor is positive, so outward upper square-root and exponential enclosures
are valid. A simultaneous reflection of both odd coefficients leaves the
norm unchanged.

\paragraph{The two coefficient choices in the certificates.}
All coefficients come from the actual marginal. For $Q=X^2-\gamma X-1$,
multiplication gives
\begin{align}\label{rescore:fourier-pearson}
 \E Q^2&=\mu_4-1-\gamma^2,&
 \E X^5&=\gamma^3+2\gamma+\E[Q^2(X+2\gamma)],\notag\\
 \mu_6&=1+3\gamma^2+\gamma^4+
       \E[Q^2\{(X+\gamma)^2+2\gamma^2+2\}].
\end{align}
For any radius $R_0\ge|X|$ with $R_0\ge2g$, the two signs of
$\E X^5-10\gamma$ are bounded, at $t=|\gamma|$, by
\begin{align*}
 P_{R_0}(t)&=R_0(M-1)+(10-2M)t-R_0t^2+t^3,\\
 Q_{R_0}(t)&=R_0(M-1)+(2M-10)t-R_0t^2-t^3.
\end{align*}
The gate $R_0-2g\ge0$ permits substituting $M$ for $\mu_4$ in both
signs. The triangle choice uses the valid enclosing radius $R_0=6$, $a_* =\max(2,M-3)/24$,
$M_6=RE_5$, $b_0=(45-M)(1-2/N)/96$ and
\begin{equation}\label{rescore:fourier-z0}
\begin{gathered}
 120Z=\min\{E_5+10g,Z_0\},\\
 Z_0=\begin{cases}
 P_6(g),&M\le5, P'_6(g)\ge0,\\
 6(M-1)+(10-2M)^2/[4(6-g)],&M\le5, P'_6(g)<0,\\
 6(M-1)+(2M-10)^2/24,&M>5.
 \end{cases}
\end{gathered}
\end{equation}
If $M\le5$, $P_6\ge Q_6$ and $P_6''<0$ on $[0,g]$; use the
increasing endpoint or $t^3\le gt^2$ and complete the square.
If $M>5$, $P_6\le6(M-1)$ and the upper quadratic for $Q_6$ gives
the third row. One may also use the actual radius by setting
$r_0=\max(R,2g)>g$ and
\[
 Z_r=\begin{cases}
 r_0(M-1)+(10-2M)t_*-(r_0-g)t_*^2,
 & M\le5,\quad t_*=\min\{g,(10-2M)/[2(r_0-g)]\},\\
 r_0(M-1)+(2M-10)t_*-r_0t_*^2,
 & M>5,\quad t_*=\min\{g,(2M-10)/(2r_0)\}.
 \end{cases}
\]
Indeed for $M\le5$, $P_{r_0}\ge Q_{r_0}$ and $t^3\le gt^2$;
for $M>5$, $P_{r_0}\le r_0(M-1)$ and dropping $-t^3$ bounds
$Q_{r_0}$. Each displayed quadratic is maximized at its clipped vertex.
Thus the selected implementation takes
$120Z=\min(E_5+10g,Z_0,Z_r)$.
It uses $c_0\ge\sqrt{\pi/(16a_0^3)}=\lambda_N$ and $c_1=c_0/2$.
The weaker choice $c_1\ge\sqrt{\pi/(256a_0^5)}
=\lambda_N N/[2(N-1)]$ is also permitted by the same transfer lemma.

For the paired choice the already proved cell has
$0\le\gamma_l\le|\gamma|\le g$, $M<3$, $g^2<2$, and $R>2g$. After reflecting only this auxiliary Fourier integral if necessary, set
\begin{gather*}
 a_*=(2-\gamma_l^2)/24,\qquad b_0=((45-M)/96)(1-2/N),\\
 M_6=\min\{RE_5,1+3g^2+g^4+(R^2+2Rg+3g^2+2)(M-1-\gamma_l^2)\},\\
 t_*=\min\{g,(10-2M)/[2(R-g)]\},\\
 120Z=\min\{E_5+10g,R(M-1)+(10-2M)t_*-(R-g)t_*^2\},\\
 z_l=\max\{-Z,[8\gamma_l-\gamma_l^3-(R+2\gamma_l)(M-1-\gamma_l^2)]/120\},
 \quad z_u=Z,\\
 c_l=(3-M)\gamma_l/144,\qquad c_u=(2-\gamma_l^2)g/144.
\end{gather*}
Check $M-1-\gamma_l^2\ge0$ and that the rectangle is ordered.
The extension from the formerly sufficient $M\le2$, $g\le1$ domain is
legitimate: Pearson gives $\mu_4\ge1+\gamma^2$, hence
$0\le a\le(2-\gamma_l^2)/24$; $M<3$ keeps $c_l\ge0$ and $g^2<2$
keeps the upper quartic coefficient nonnegative. The general transfer
hypotheses remain true since $M<3<11.1$ and $g<\sqrt2<1.85$.
The damping must be $(45-M)(1-2/N)/96$; the older value
$43(1-2/N)/96$ is not licensed when $M>2$. Here
$P_R\ge Q_R$, and $P_R(t)\le R(M-1)+(10-2M)t-(R-g)t^2$,
whose maximum is at $t_*$. The numerator of the lower $z$ bound increases
in $\gamma\ge0$ because its derivative is
$10-2M+2R\gamma+3\gamma^2>0$. Pearson's first and third identities
give $a_*$ and $M_6$. If $3g^2\le2$, the increasing function
$2\gamma-\gamma^3$ further licenses $c_u\le(2g-g^3)/144$.
Thus this rectangle is a specialization of the signed transfer on the
whole enlarged domain. Its actual moment inputs, including any shared
lower bound $r_*\le\rho$, are acquired before it is used. The signed
phase calculation keeps the original $\gamma$; reflection here acts only
on the auxiliary Fourier norm and both its odd coefficients.

\subsection{A finite-window LOW bound at the actual index}
\label{rescore:sec:finite-low}

The preceding LOW bounds extend positive Gaussian majorants to infinity.
At small indices this can be wasteful. The following alternative keeps
the original integration interval; it uses no physical-gap premise.

\begin{lemma}[Finite-window transfer]\label{rescore:lem:finite-low}
Suppose $n>2$ is an integer, $1\le R\le6$, $|X|\le R$,
$\mu_4\le M\le111/10$, $|\gamma|\le g\le37/20$,
and $\eta_5\le E_5$. Let $0\le g_l\le|\gamma|$ with $g_l^2\le M-1$.
Set
\begin{gather*}
 M_6=\min\{RE_5,
 1+3g^2+g^4+(R^2+2Rg+3g^2+2)(M-1-g_l^2)\},\\
 A_*\ge |(3-\mu_4)/24|,\quad
 Z\ge|(10\gamma-\E X^5)/120|,\\
 E=M_6/720+g^2/72+(1/2+g^2/144)^3/5,\qquad
 B=A_*+Z/2+E/4,\\
 a_n=(n-1)/2,\qquad b_n=(n-2)(45-M)/96,\qquad
 I_j(A)=\int_0^{1/2}t^je^{-At^2}\,dt.
\end{gather*}
Then $I_0(H)$ is bounded by $(L_0+L_H|H|)/\sqrt n+L_2/n$, with
the following complete triple:
\begin{align}
 L_0={}&\sqrt n\{nZI_2(a_n)
       +n(n-1)A_*gI_4(a_n)/6+n^3g^3I_6(n/2)/1296\},\nonumber\\
 L_H={}&\sqrt n\{nA_*I_2(a_n)+n^2g^2I_4(n/2)/72\},\nonumber\\
 L_2={}&n\{nEI_3(a_n)+n(n-1)B^2I_5(b_n)/2\}.
 \label{rescore:eq:finite-low-triple}
\end{align}
The bound holds for every real raw threshold $H$.
One always may use
$A_*=\max\{|3-M|,|2-g_l^2|\}/24$ and $Z=(E_5+10g)/120$;
the sharper proved Pearson coefficient bounds above may be substituted.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Pearson's sixth-moment identity
\eqref{rescore:fourier-pearson}, whose residual multiplier is positive,
gives the stated $M_6$; also $\mu_6\le R\eta_5$ pointwise.
Put $a=(3-\mu_4)/24$ and $z=(10\gamma-\E X^5)/120$.
For $h(t)=e^{-t^2/2-i\gamma t^3/6}$,
\[
 f(t)-h(t)=-at^4-izt^5+r_6(t),\qquad |r_6(t)|\le Et^6
 \quad(0\le t\le1/2).
\]
Indeed, with $w=-t^2/2-i\gamma t^3/6$,
$|w|\le t^2(1/2+g^2/144)<1/6$. Comparing with $1+w+w^2/2$
costs $g^2t^6/72+|w|^3e^{|w|}/6\le
\{g^2/72+(1/2+g^2/144)^3/5\}t^6$, besides the true sixth-moment
remainder $\mu_6t^6/720$. Thus $|f-h|\le Bt^4$ on the whole interval.

Symmetrization gives
\[
 |f(t)|^2\le1-t^2+(M+3)t^4/12
 \le1-(45-M)t^2/48.
\]
Both $|f(t)|$ and $|h(t)|$ are therefore at most
$e^{-(45-M)t^2/96}$, as is every point on their complex line segment.
Taylor's integral formula for the polynomial $z^n$ yields
\[
 f^n-h^n=n(f-h)h^{n-1}+R_2,\qquad
 |R_2|\le\frac{n(n-1)}2B^2t^8e^{-b_nt^2}.
\]
The $r_6$ and $R_2$ terms, after division by $t^3$ and integration,
cost $nEI_3(a_n)+n(n-1)B^2I_5(b_n)/2$.
The leading imaginary part is at most
\[
 ne^{-a_nt^2}\{(Z+A_*|H|)t^5+(n-1)A_*gt^7/6\}.
\]
Finally, for $u=n\gamma t^3/6$,
\[
 |\Im[e^{-itH}(h^n-e_n)]|
 \le e^{-nt^2/2}\{|u|^3/6+|H|t u^2/2\}.
\]
Integrating these terms gives every coefficient in
\eqref{rescore:eq:finite-low-triple}, with no replacement of $n(n-1)$
by $n^2$ and no omitted frequency interval.
\end{proof}

For rational $A\ge0$, exact enclosures for these finite integrals are
obtained from
\begin{equation}\label{rescore:eq:finite-gaussian-integral}
 P_{m,j}(A)=\sum_{k=0}^m
 \frac{(-A)^k}{k!(j+2k+1)2^{j+2k+1}},\qquad
 \max\{0,P_{21,j}(A)\}\le I_j(A)\le P_{20,j}(A).
\end{equation}
The integral Taylor remainder of $e^{-x}$ has sign $(-1)^{m+1}$
for every $x\ge0$, not merely on an alternating-series domain.
Integrate its polynomial bounds after $x=At^2$.
An implementation restricted to $A\in[0,8]$ and $0\le j\le16$
must check those gates before using this particular evaluator.
Every weight in \eqref{rescore:eq:finite-low-triple} is nonnegative.
An alternative LOW choice is always one entire valid triple; taking
coordinatewise minima of triples is not justified.

\subsection{The complete high-frequency integral}
For $b,\kappa>0$ and $n>0$, put
\[
 T_n=\sqrt n\int_b^\infty e^{-n\kappa t^2}t^{-3}\,dt,
 \qquad x=n\kappa b^2.
\]
\begin{lemma}[HIGH\_LAGUERRE2]
For every $x>0$,
\[
 T_n\le\frac{\sqrt n e^{-x}}{2b^2}
                  \frac{x+2}{x^2+4x+2}.
\]
If $N\kappa b^2\ge1/4$, the right side decreases with $n\ge N$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Substitute $t^2=b^2(1+s)$ and use positive Fubini to obtain
\[
 T_n=\frac{\sqrt n e^{-x}}{2b^2}
 \int_0^\infty\frac{e^{-xs}}{(1+s)^2}\,ds
 =\frac{\sqrt n e^{-x}}{2b^2}
 \int_0^\infty\frac{u}{x+u}e^{-u}\,du.
\]
Put $P(u)=u^2-4u+2$ and $D(x)=x^2+4x+2=P(-x)>0$.
Exact division gives
\[
 \frac1{x+u}-\frac{Q_x(u)}{D(x)^2}
       =\frac{P(u)^2}{(x+u)D(x)^2}\ge0,
\]
where
\[
 Q_x(u)=-u^3+(x+8)u^2-(x^2+8x+20)u
                  +x^3+8x^2+20x+16.
\]
Integration uses only $\int_0^\infty u^j e^{-u}du=j!$, proved by
integration by parts. It gives
$\int Q_x(u)e^{-u}du=(x+3)D(x)$ and hence
\[
 \int_0^\infty\frac{u}{x+u}e^{-u}du
 =1-x\int_0^\infty\frac{e^{-u}}{x+u}du
 \le1-\frac{x(x+3)}{D(x)}=\frac{x+2}{D(x)}.
\]
For the all-index statement, differentiate
$\sqrt{x}e^{-x}(x+2)/D(x)$. After multiplication by positive factors,
its derivative has numerator
$4-10x-22x^2-13x^3-2x^4$. This polynomial is negative at $x=1/4$
and strictly decreasing for $x>0$. Thus it is negative on the entire
required half-line.
\end{proof}


Let $EX=0$, $EX^2=1$, $EX^4\le M$, and write
$\gamma=EX^3$, $f(t)=Ee^{itX}$. For independent copies suppose an
actual event $E$ satisfies
\[
 P(E)\ge a>0,\qquad |X-X'|\ge d_0>0\text{ on }E,\qquad
 E[(X-X')^2\mid E]\le M_E.
\]
The event is measured under the product of the same marginal law.
Define the Taylor comparator
$e_n(t)=e^{-nt^2/2}(1-in\gamma t^3/6)$ and
\[
 I_\infty=\int_{1/2}^{\infty}|f(t)^n-e_n(t)|t^{-3}\,dt,
 \qquad L(x)=\frac{x+2}{x^2+4x+2}.
\]
The sign of the imaginary term is immaterial for its absolute-value bound.
Use $b=9/10$, $0<r\le1/4$,
\begin{gather*}
 h=\frac{333}{106}-\frac{(22/7)^3r^2}{6}>0,\quad
 \kappa=a(23/48)^2d_0^2,\\
 S_3=\sum_{j=1}^{20}j^{-3}+\frac1{800},\qquad
 S_5=\sum_{j=1}^{20}(j-r)^{-5}+\frac1{4(20-r)^4}.
\end{gather*}
Set
\[
 \begin{gathered}c=\frac{1-\max_{u\in\{1/4,81/100\}}
              [1-u+(M+3)u^2/12]}2,\\
 c_0=\frac18-\frac{M+3}{384},\quad
 s=\frac{1-(M+3)53/600}{2}.\end{gathered}
\]
The usual damping domain requires $c>0$.

\begin{lemma}[HIGH\_WINDOW50]
For every integer $n\ge5$, the complete upper bound is
\begin{align*}
 \sqrt n I_\infty\le{}& A_n
 +\frac{M_E}{4\pi^{3/2}h\sqrt a}
       \left(S_3+\frac{3S_5}{nah^2}\right)
 +\frac{M_E\sqrt n}{2\pi^2}e^{-nah^2r^2}\\
 &+Z_n+\frac{\sqrt n}{2b^2}e^{-na(23/48)^2}
 +\sqrt n e^{-n/8}\left(2L(n/8)+\frac{\sqrt{M-1}}3\right),
\end{align*}
where always $A_n=2\sqrt n e^{-nc}$ is permitted, and, if $s>0$,
it can be replaced by the smaller of itself and
\[
 2\sqrt n e^{-nc_0}L(ns/4).
\]
The zero-cell term $Z_n$ may be taken as the smaller of
\[
 \frac{e^{-n\kappa b^2}}{2b^4\kappa\sqrt n},\qquad
 \frac{\sqrt n e^{-n\kappa b^2}}{2b^2}L(n\kappa b^2).
\]
Each term may be evaluated at a common floor $N$ for all $n\ge N$
provided
\[
 2Nc'>1\quad(c'\in\{c,ah^2r^2,a(23/48)^2,1/8\}).
\]
For the new first term also require $2Nc_0>1$;
for the second zero-cell option require $N\kappa b^2\ge1/4$.
If either extra condition is unavailable, retain the corresponding simpler term.
Any fixed collection of permitted windows may be evaluated, and the minimum
of its complete six-term bounds remains valid.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
We give the entire frequency partition to make clear what changes.
On $[1/2,b]$, symmetrization and
$\cos u\le1-u^2/2+u^4/24$ yield
\[
 |f(t)|^2\le1-t^2+(M+3)t^4/12,
 \qquad |f(t)|^n\le e^{-n c(t^2)},
 \quad c(u)=u/2-(M+3)u^2/24.
\]
Bounding $c(t^2)$ below by $c$ and using
$\int_{1/2}^{b}t^{-3}dt<2$ gives the first permitted term.
Concavity supplies the more precise chord bound
\[
 c(u)\ge c_0+s(u-1/4),\qquad 1/4\le u\le81/100,
\]
because the difference is exactly
$(M+3)(u-1/4)(81/100-u)/24$. If $s>0$, extend only the resulting
positive exponential majorant to the half-line. The preceding rational
Gaussian-tail lemma, with lower endpoint $1/2$, gives
\[
 \sqrt n\int_{1/2}^{b}e^{-nc(t^2)}t^{-3}dt
 \le e^{-n(c_0-s/4)}\sqrt n
       \int_{1/2}^{\infty}e^{-ns t^2}t^{-3}dt
 \le2\sqrt n e^{-nc_0}L(ns/4).
\]

For $t\ge b$, conditioning on the actual event and convex Jensen give
\[
 |f(t)|^n\le e^{-nE\sin^2(t(X-X')/2)}
 \le E[e^{-na\sin^2(td/2)}\mid E],\qquad d=|X-X'|.
\]
Positive Tonelli allows a separate partition for every $d>0$.
Put $\ell=2\pi/d$. The zero cell is $[0,\ell/2]$; the other cells are
$\ell[j-1/2,j+1/2]$, $j\ge1$. In each nonzero cell take the core
$t=\ell(j+v)$, $|v|\le r$. These cores are disjoint even for $r\le1/2$.
For our smaller range $r\le1/4$, the elementary sine bound gives
$|\sin(\pi v)|\ge h|v|$ throughout a core, and
$|\sin(\pi v)|\ge hr$ throughout its wings. The latter uses that
$\sin(\pi|v|)$ increases on $[0,1/2]$.

Since $j-r>0$, Taylor's formula on the two sides gives
\[
 \frac{(j-v)^{-3}+(j+v)^{-3}}2
 \le j^{-3}+6(j-r)^{-5}v^2.
\]
Integrate this majorant against $e^{-nah^2v^2}$ on the full real line.
The result is
$\sqrt\pi[j^{-3}/\sqrt{nah^2}
  +3(j-r)^{-5}/(nah^2)^{3/2}]$.
The Jacobian is
$t^{-3}dt=d^2(4\pi^2)^{-1}(j+v)^{-3}dv$.
Summing and averaging $d^2$ gives the second term. The two series tails
are bounded by their decreasing integrals after $20$, producing exactly
$S_3,S_5$. The total wing weight is at most
$\int_{\ell/2}^{\infty}t^{-3}dt=d^2/(2\pi^2)$, giving the third term.
Extending cores or wings below $b$ only adds a positive integral.

In the zero cell write $u=td/2\le\pi/2$.
For $u\le1/2$, use $\sin u\ge(23/24)u$ and the rational
Gaussian-tail lemma with rate $\kappa$; its two choices give $Z_n$.
For $u\ge1/2$, use $\sin u\ge23/48$ and total weight
$1/(2b^2)$, giving the fifth term. Shared endpoints have zero integration
measure, including when the conditional distance distribution has atoms.

Finally keep the comparator on the entire interval $[1/2,\infty)$.
Its absolute-value bound splits its integral into
\[
 \sqrt n\int_{1/2}^{\infty}e^{-nt^2/2}t^{-3}dt
 +\frac{n^{3/2}|\gamma|}{6}
                 \int_{1/2}^{\infty}e^{-nt^2/2}dt.
\]
The first part is at most $2\sqrt n e^{-n/8}L(n/8)$.
For the second, $t\ge1/2$ implies
\[
 \int_{1/2}^{\infty}e^{-nt^2/2}dt
 \le2\int_{1/2}^{\infty}t e^{-nt^2/2}dt
 =2e^{-n/8}/n.
\]
Pearson's elementary identity
$E[(X^2-1-\gamma X)^2]=EX^4-1-\gamma^2\ge0$
gives $|\gamma|\le\sqrt{M-1}$ and proves the sixth term.
Any separately proved upper bound $|\gamma|\le g$ may replace
$\sqrt{M-1}$ in that term. The selected implementation uses
$g=\min(1.85,\sqrt{M-1})$.

For uniformity, $\sqrt n e^{-nc'}$ decreases when $2nc'>1$,
whereas $n^{-1}$ and $n^{-1/2}e^{-nc'}$ always decrease.
The derivative of $L(x)$ is
$-(x^2+4x+6)/(x^2+4x+2)^2<0$.
The new first term is thus decreasing under its stated gate, and the
comparator decreases for every $n\ge5$. The earlier tail lemma proves
the separate $N\kappa b^2\ge1/4$ gate for its new zero-cell option.
These comparisons concern the bounding expressions, so they are uniform
even when the marginal law varies with $n$.
\end{proof}\paragraph{Events, not artificial kernels.}\label{rescore:fourier-events}
The entry uses only the following actual choices, with the direct rate
from the same fourth moment in each case:
\[
\begin{array}{c|c|c|c}
 \text{event}&a&d_0&M_E\\\hline
 (X-X')^2\ge9/25 &(2-9/25)^2/(2M+6)&3/5&2/a\\
 XX'<0, D\le d\le.8 &1/(2r_*^2)&\sqrt{2-d}&4r_*^2\\
 XX'<0, p\in[l,u] &2\min(l(1-l),u(1-u))
 &\max(A_l+B_l,\sqrt{2-d})&2/a
\end{array}
\]
The first row follows from Cauchy using
$\E[(X-X')^2;E]\ge2-d_0^2$ and
$\E(X-X')^4=2\mu_4+6$. The second is Lemma~\ref{rescore:entry201-event}; all its moment and $\alpha<3$ gates are checked at use.
In the third, $D\le d<2$ ensures a genuine central gap,
$P(XX'<0)=2pq$, and restriction of the squared distance gives $M_E=2/a$.
Taking a minimum of \emph{complete} high-frequency bounds is legitimate. No tail is
dropped and the third construction also allows a cell crossing $1/2$.

% SOURCE: entry/resource_enclosure.tex
\subsection{Keeping compensation inside supported HIGH}
