Thus $u=0$ at such a maximum and $C=C_B$. Schulz's all-probability
Bernoulli theorem~\cite[Theorem~1]{Schulz2016} gives $C_B<C_E$ for every
$0<p<1$ and in particular for all the indices $n\ge47$ used here. The same
external theorem closes the positive-noise conclusion $C<C_B<C_E$; no
finite Bernoulli reserve is needed. No estimate
in this section divides by zero or passes from fixed-law convergence to
uniformity in varying laws.

A0. NORMALIZATION-CROSS-HALF-PROOF-v1.txt
Original ordinary proof: work/final-audit/NORMALIZATION-CROSS-HALF-PROOF-v1.txt
Use in main: Appendix normalization: crossing-segment binomial mass envelope
============================================================================

Normalization on a binomial Taylor segment that may cross one half
=================================================================

Ordinary mathematical proof supplement for the final independent audit.
No checker, numerical result, accepted statement, or proof identifier changes.

Source alignment
----------------
The original source manuscript, subsection "Normalization and the repair,
once for every witness" (lines8318 onward), states a sufficient recipe with
r_+ < 1/2. The active normalization.py with whole SHA
3525584b3cf0e9d98b6d3408705f85648bc644081a682e5b6904cac084181603
instead permits right>1/2 under the stronger left-end dominance condition
left+right<1. This supplement proves exactly that extension. The original
probability band p in[p_-,p_+] remains strictly below1/2 throughout.

Lemma (symmetric derivative envelopes).
Let0<a<1/2, a<b<1, and a+b<1. For every r in[a,b],

  r(1-r) >= a(1-a),
  |1-2r|/[r^2(1-r)^2] <= (1-2a)/[a^2(1-a)^2],
  |1-2r|/[r(1-r)] <= (1-2a)/[a(1-a)],
  1/[12r(1-r)] <= 1/[12a(1-a)].

Proof. The assumption b<1-a gives[a,b] subset[a,1-a]. For
s=min(r,1-r) we therefore have a<=s<=1/2. Each displayed expression
is invariant under r->1-r. On(0,1/2], s(1-s) is increasing, while
1-2s is nonnegative and decreasing. Consequently the first inequality
holds, and each nonnegative quotient is at most its value at a: its
numerator decreases and its positive denominator increases. The final
inequality follows from the first one. This includes r=1/2 and either
side of that point. No assumption b<1/2 has been used. QED.

Corollary (the same normalization recipe).
Let n>=N>=1 be integers, p in[p_-,p_+] subset(0,1/2), q=1-p,
and let k be an integer with1<=k<=n-1. Set

  delta=k-np, r=k/n, v=pq,
  D(r||p)=r log(r/p)+(1-r)log((1-r)/q).

Suppose |delta|<=Delta and the complete segment between p and r lies
inside[a,b], where a,b satisfy the lemma. Let E,L,R be positive numbers
satisfying

  E >= (1-2a)/[a^2(1-a)^2],
  L >= (1-2a)/[a(1-a)],
  R >= 1/[12a(1-a)].

Then the binomial mass pi_k=binomial(n,k)p^k q^(n-k) has the exact form

  sqrt(n) pi_k = phi(0)/sqrt(v) * exp(-delta^2/(2nv)+zeta),
  n|zeta| < Z(|delta|),
  Z(t)=Lt/2+R+Et^3/(6N).

Proof. The identities

  D(p||p)=D'(p||p)=0, D''(p||p)=1/v,
  D'''(u||p)=(2u-1)/[u^2(1-u)^2],
  (log(u(1-u)))'=(1-2u)/[u(1-u)]

and the lemma give, by Taylor's theorem and the mean value theorem,

  |nD(r||p)-delta^2/(2nv)| <= E|delta|^3/(6n^2),
  |log(r(1-r))-log(v)| <= L|delta|/n.

Use the finite Stirling formula

  log(j!)=(j+1/2)log(j)-j+log(2pi)/2+s_j,
  0<s_j<1/(12j), j>=1.

It gives

  sqrt(n) pi_k = phi(0)/sqrt(r(1-r))
                 *exp(-nD(r||p)+s_n-s_k-s_(n-k)).

The factorial error has the stated bound without adding incompatible
one-sided estimates: writing e=s_n-s_k-s_(n-k),

  e < s_n < 1/(12n) <= 1/[12nr(1-r)],
  e > -s_k-s_(n-k) > -1/[12nr(1-r)].

Hence |e|<R/n by the lemma. Collecting this error, the entropy remainder,
and minus one half the logarithmic prefactor error into zeta gives

  n|zeta| < L|delta|/2+R+E|delta|^3/(6n)
           <= Z(|delta|),

as required. The strict factorial remainder suffices for the strict
inequality; the outward-rounded code gates supply additional margins. QED.

Literal application to the active code
------------------------------------
The code sets a=left and b=right, and checks

  0<left<1/2, left<right<1, left+right<1,
  left < p_- - Delta/N,
  p_+ + Delta/N < right.

For n>=N and |delta|<=Delta these inequalities put p and k/n, and the
whole segment between them, inside[left,right]. The exact positive gates
N*p_- - Delta -2>0 and N*(1-p_+) - Delta -2>0 ensure the required interior
count/factorial domains, with room for the adjacent counts used by the
other estimates. Thus there is no factorial endpoint case hidden in this
use of Stirling's formula.

The generated vf is strictly positive and smaller than left*(1-left).
The upward-rounded bounds

  entropy > (1-2left)/vf^2,
  logder > (1-2left)/vf,
  remainder > 1/(12vf)

are therefore valid E,L,R in the corollary. In particular they remain
valid when right>1/2. Nothing else in the mass derivation depends on
which side of1/2 the intermediate Taylor point lies.

The lower and upper binomial-mass consequences in the source recipe now
follow unchanged from this same zeta envelope, their stated positivity
gates, and exp(-x)>=1-x and exp(x)<=1/(1-x) for0<=x<1. When using the
factor sqrt(pq), the actual p band remains below1/2, so pq<=p_+(1-p_+)
is still valid. No such monotonicity is assumed for the larger r segment.
For an actual binomial mode, |k-np|<=1, so Delta>=1 includes it even if
its Taylor segment crosses1/2.

The proof extends the exact normalization envelope only. It does not
grant an incoming sharp bound, same-law resource hypothesis, numerical
exclusion, or all-law conclusion by itself. All those original inputs
remain separately mandatory in the current source-mathematical theorem.

A1. prawitz_majorant_review.md
Original ordinary proof: work/berry-esseen-end2end-lean-map/proof/prawitz_majorant_review.md
Use in main: Fourier and ROOT4: exact pointwise closed-half-line kernel and compensated identity
============================================================================

## 1. A real-variable Beurling construction

Set

\[
 W(x)=\left(\frac{\sin\pi x}{\pi x}\right)^2\quad(x\ne0),
 \qquad W(0)=1,
\]

and define the continuous odd function

\[
 H_B(x)=\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\{W(x-m)-W(x+m)\}+2xW(x).
 \tag{1}
\]

The series converges absolutely and locally uniformly on the real line:
for \(|x|\le R\) and \(m>2R+1\), each shifted term is bounded by a
constant times \(m^{-2}\). Thus the function and all the integer values in
what follows are unambiguous. In particular,

\[
 H_B(0)=0,\qquad H_B(j)=\operatorname{sgn}(j)
 \quad(j\in\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\}).                 \tag{2}
\]

Here \(\operatorname{sgn}(0)=0\). The corresponding classical Beurling
majorant is \(B=H_B+W\). For noninteger \(x\), its series is

\[
 B(x)=\left(\frac{\sin\pi x}{\pi}\right)^2
 \left\{\frac2x+\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac1{(x-m)^2}
                    -\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\frac1{(x+m)^2}\right\}.
 \tag{3}
\]

We prove the needed inequality directly, without importing an extremality
or Fourier-transform theorem about \(B\).

The elementary partial-fraction identity

\[
 \sum_{m\in\mathbb Z}\frac1{(y-m)^2}
       =\frac{\pi^2}{\sin^2\pi y},\qquad y\notin\mathbb Z,
 \tag{4}
\]

has the following short proof. Integrate
\(\pi\cot(\pi z)/(z-y)^2\) counterclockwise over the square with vertices
\(\pm(N+\tfrac12)\pm i(N+\tfrac12)\). The cotangent is uniformly bounded
on these edges: on the vertical edges it is a multiple of a hyperbolic
tangent, and on the horizontal edges its modulus is bounded by
\(\coth(\pi(N+\tfrac12))\). The denominator is of order \(N^2\) and the
perimeter of order \(N\), so the integral tends to zero. Its residues are
\((m-y)^{-2}\) at \(m=-N,\ldots,N\), and
\(-\pi^2\csc^2(\pi y)\) at \(y\). The residue theorem and absolute
convergence give (4).

For \(y>0\), \(y\notin\mathbb Z\), put

\[
 A(y)=\left(\frac{\sin\pi y}{\pi}\right)^2,
 \qquad S(y)=\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}(y+m)^{-2}.
\]

Subtracting (4) from (1) gives exactly

\[
 H_B(y)-1=A(y)\left\{\frac2y-\frac1{y^2}-2S(y)\right\}.
 \tag{5}
\]

The decreasing positive function \(s\mapsto(y+s)^{-2}\) satisfies

\[
 S(y)\le\int_0^\infty(y+s)^{-2}\,ds=\frac1y
       \le\frac1{y^2}+S(y).
 \tag{6}
\]

Consequently the expression in braces in (5) lies between
\(-y^{-2}\) and \(y^{-2}\). Hence

\[
 |H_B(y)-1|\le W(y)\quad(y>0).
\]

Positive integer points follow directly from (2). Oddness of \(H_B\) and
evenness of \(W\), together with their values at zero, now prove

\[
 |H_B(x)-\operatorname{sgn}(x)|\le W(x)
 \qquad(x\in\mathbb R).                              \tag{7}
\]

This is the required sign-function majorization, with its normalization
proved rather than assumed.

## 2. Recovering the exact Fourier kernel

Elementary integration gives, for every real \(x\),

\[
 W(x)=2\int_0^1(1-t)\cos(2\pi tx)\,dt.              \tag{8}
\]

For \(0<r<1\), insert (8) into the absolutely convergent Abel-weighted
series. Termwise integration is legitimate because
\(\sum_{m\ge1}r^m<\infty\). The cosine subtraction formula gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \sum_{m\ge1}r^m\{W(x-m)-W(x+m)\}
   &=4\int_0^1(1-t)\sin(2\pi tx)
               \sum_{m\ge1}r^m\sin(2\pi mt)\,dt\\
   &=4\int_0^1(1-t)\sin(2\pi tx)
        \frac{r\sin(2\pi t)}{1-2r\cos(2\pi t)+r^2}\,dt.
 \end{aligned}                                                    \tag{9}
\]

For \(0<t<1\),

\[
 \left|\frac{r\sin(2\pi t)}{1-2r\cos(2\pi t)+r^2}\right|
 \le\frac12|\cot(\pi t)|,
 \tag{10}
\]

since the denominator is
\((1-r)^2+4r\sin^2(\pi t)\). The right side of (9), including its
remaining factors, is therefore dominated by

\[
 2(1-t)|\sin(2\pi tx)|\,|\cot(\pi t)|.
\]

This is integrable at both endpoints: the sine is \(O_x(t)\) at zero,
and \((1-t)\cot(\pi t)\) is bounded at one. Dominated convergence on
the integral side and absolute convergence on the series side allow
\(r\uparrow1\). The fraction in (9) tends to
\(\tfrac12\cot(\pi t)\). Also, direct integration gives

\[
 2xW(x)=\frac2\pi\int_0^1\sin(2\pi tx)\,dt,          \tag{11}
\]

including \(x=0\). Combining (1) and (9)–(11),

\[
 H_B(x)=2\int_0^1 R(t)\sin(2\pi tx)\,dt,
 \qquad R(t)=(1-t)\cot(\pi t)+\frac1\pi.
 \tag{12}
\]

Every integral in (12) is a convergent real integral. No principal-value
Fourier manipulation has been used.

Now take exactly the manuscript's kernel

\[
 K(t)=\frac{1-t}{2}+\frac i2 R(t),\qquad0<t<1.
 \tag{13}
\]

Equations (8) and (12) establish the exact identity

\[
 \boxed{\displaystyle
 M(x):=\frac12+2\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow0}\operatorname{Re}
       \int_\epsilon^1K(t)e^{2\pi itx}\,dt
       =\frac{1-H_B(x)+W(x)}2=\frac{1+B(-x)}2.}
 \tag{14}
\]

In particular, the exponent has the **positive** sign and the imaginary
part of \(K\) has the **positive** sign. The minus sign on \(H_B\) comes
from taking the real part of \(iR(t)e^{2\pi itx}\).

## 3. Closed boundary, cardinal values and the minorant

For \(x<0\), (7) gives \(M(x)\ge1\); for \(x>0\), it gives
\(M(x)\ge0\). At the boundary, (2) and \(W(0)=1\) give

\[
 M(0)=1.                                             \tag{15}
\]

Thus this majorizes the **closed** half-line, including a point mass at
zero, rather than merely the half-height convention for a jump:

\[
 M(x)\ge1_{(-\infty,0]}(x),\qquad
 1-M(-x)\le1_{(-\infty,0)}(x).
 \tag{16}
\]

The second inequality follows by subtracting the first inequality at
\(-x\) from one; its endpoint is open. Direct cardinal values are

\[
 M(j)=1_{\{j\le0\}},\qquad1-M(-j)=1_{\{j<0\}}
 \quad(j\in\mathbb Z).                              \tag{17}
\]

The proof also gives the useful global error bound

\[
 0\le M(x)-1_{(-\infty,0]}(x)\le W(x)
 \quad(x\in\mathbb R).                              \tag{18}
\]

At zero the left-hand error is exactly zero; it is not obtained by
assigning half of an atom to each side. In particular \(0\le M\le2\).

## 4. Expectations and the compensated identity

Suppose \(U\) is real and \(\mathbb E|U|<\infty\), and use the convention
\(\psi_U(t)=\mathbb E e^{2\pi itU}\). Since
\(2\int_0^1\operatorname{Re}K(t)\,dt=1/2\), (14) can be written

\[
 M(u)=1+2\operatorname{Re}\int_0^1
             K(t)\{e^{2\pi itu}-1\}\,dt.             \tag{19}
\]

The complex integral in (19) is absolutely convergent. Indeed,
\(K(t)=i/(2\pi t)+1/2+O(t)\) at zero, \(K\) is bounded near one,
and

\[
 \mathbb E|e^{2\pi itU}-1|\le2\pi t\,\mathbb E|U|.
\]

Consequently
\(\int_0^1|K(t)|\mathbb E|e^{2\pi itU}-1|\,dt<\infty\), so ordinary
absolute Fubini applies to (19). Restoring the constant real part gives

\[
 \mathbb EM(U)=\frac12+
     2\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow0}\operatorname{Re}
        \int_\epsilon^1K(t)\psi_U(t)\,dt.            \tag{20}
\]

Since \(\psi_{-U}=\overline{\psi_U}\), conjugation similarly yields

\[
 \mathbb E\{1-M(-U)\}=\frac12-
     2\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow0}\operatorname{Re}
        \int_\epsilon^1\overline{K(t)}\psi_U(t)\,dt.
 \tag{21}
\]

These are precisely the two identities (P4) under the real-part
improper convention (P0). All expectations are finite; (18) even gives
boundedness of the majorant without a moment assumption. The first
absolute moment is a sufficient hypothesis for the stated compensated
Fourier/Fubini argument. The uncompensated complex integral itself need
not converge.

## 5. Scale and sign in the physical ROOT4 formula

Let \(Y\) be integrable, let \(h\in\mathbb R\), and let \(T>0\). For
\(\chi_Y(s)=\mathbb E e^{isY}\), apply (16) and (20) to
\(U=T(Y-h)/(2\pi)\). The resulting exact normalization is

\[
 \mathbb P(Y\le h)\le\frac12+
  2\lim_{\epsilon\downarrow0}\operatorname{Re}
       \int_\epsilon^1K(t)e^{-iTth}\chi_Y(Tt)\,dt.
 \tag{22}
\]

For the v4 ROOT4 setting,
\(Z=(X_1+\cdots+X_4)/2\), \(\varphi_P(s)=\mathbb E e^{isX_1}\),
and iid summands, choose \(T=6\). Independence gives
\(\chi_Z(6t)=\varphi_P(3t)^4\). Thus (22) is exactly

\[
 F_Z(x)\le\frac12+\int_0^1\operatorname{Re}
   \{\widehat K(t)e^{-6itx}\varphi_P(3t)^4\}\,dt,
 \qquad \widehat K=2K,
 \tag{23}
\]

the manuscript's `n4all:eq:prawitz`. Writing \(D=x-Z\) and
\(S(t)=tR(t)\), its real integrand is exactly

\[
 (1-t)\mathbb E\cos(6Dt)+S(t)\mathbb E[6D\operatorname{sinc}(6Dt)],
 \qquad \operatorname{sinc}(v)=\sin(v)/v,
 \tag{24}
\]

with the continuous value at zero. This verifies both the factor six
and the sign of the sine term. No non-atomicity, finite-support, density,
or continuity-at-the-threshold assumption is needed. For (23), iid
independence and integrability are sufficient; the manuscript's bounded
support assumptions imply integrability.

A2. logic_gaussian_input_audit.md
Original ordinary proof: work/berry-esseen-end2end-lean-map/proof/logic_gaussian_input_audit.md
Use in main: Support: direct Gaussian bound on both complete far rays
============================================================================

## Direct check of the selected estimate

Write u=z-y/sqrt(n) and f(lambda)=Phi(u/sqrt(1-lambda)). Taylor expansion
of Phi at z through order two leaves a translation remainder bounded by
phi(0)|y|^3/6 after multiplication by n^(3/2), since
`sup |phi''| = phi(0)`.

Taylor expansion of f at zero gives the remaining main term
sqrt(n)[u phi(u)-z phi(z)]/2. Its absolute value is at most
phi(0)|y|/2 < |y|/5 because `(x phi(x))'=-phi''(x)`.

For 0 <= lambda <= 1/2, putting r=u/sqrt(1-lambda),

\[
 f''(\lambda)=\frac{r(3-r^2)\phi(r)}{4(1-\lambda)^2}.
\]

The bounds `sup |r| phi(r)<1/4` and `sup |r|^3 phi(r)<1/2` imply
`sup |f''|<5/4`. Thus the multiplied scale remainder is less than
5/(8 sqrt(n)) <= 5/(8 sqrt(2)) < 1/2. Summing these three bounds proves
the displayed estimate on the full stated domain.

This discharges the identified statement/normalization concern for this input.
It does not validate support-row arithmetic, the later compensated Fourier
inequalities, the uniform local comparison, or their implementations.

A3. GENERAL_PAIRED_PROOF.txt
Original ordinary proof: work/qualified-replay-source/snapshot/vendor/finite1014_core_documents/GENERAL_PAIRED_PROOF.txt
Use in main: Finite paired Fourier/resource applications: signed Pearson domain and coefficient rectangles
============================================================================

Signed paired coefficient extension
===================================
Status: locally derived conditional analytic lemma extending coefficient acquisition in the retained signed Gaussian transfer. The accompanying numerical implementation is FLOAT discovery only. No new certificate or cutoff follows without exact enclosure and complete acceptance.

Scope. EX=0, EX²=1, |X|<=R, mu4<=M<=11.1, |gamma|<=g<=1.85, E|X|5<=eta, and0<=gl<=|gamma| with M>=1+gl². R>=1 suffices; neither R>2g nor R>g is needed by the formulas below. The already proved signed Gaussian transfer still has its separate n>2 and positive damping hypotheses.

Reflect only the auxiliary Fourier integral if necessary so t=gamma=|gamma|>=0. The original signed gamma used in the contact phase identity is unchanged. Put Q=X²-tX-1 and v=EQ²=mu4-1-t²>=0. Exact multiplication gives
 mu5=t³+2t+E[Q²(X+2t)],
 mu6=1+3t²+t⁴+E[Q²((X+t)²+2t²+2)].
Thus for z=(10t-mu5)/120 and c=(3-mu4)t/144,
 L(t)<=120z<=U(t),
 L(t)=8t-t³-(R+2t)(M-1-t²),
 U(t)=8t-t³+(R-2t)_+(M-1-t²),
for every feasible actual t in[gl,g]. The second inequality uses v<=M-1-t² when R-2t>=0; when R-2t<0 it instead uses v>=0. This sign switch is the precise reason the old simultaneous substitution of M required R>=2g.

For coefficient enclosure, L is the cubic
 -R(M-1)+(10-2M)t+Rt²+t³.
Its minimum over[gl,g] is at gl when M<=5. Generally include both endpoints and real derivative roots of3t²+2Rt+10-2M inside the interval. For U, split at R/2: use
 P(t)=R(M-1)+(10-2M)t-Rt²+t³ on[gl,min(g,R/2)],
 b(t)=8t-t³ on[max(gl,R/2),g].
On each nonempty subinterval include both endpoints and its real derivative roots. Alternatively exact rational Bernstein bounds on a closed subdivision give certified bounds without algebraic-root arithmetic. Infeasible t with t²>M-1 may be retained: enclosures are taken over a superset, while the underlying pointwise inequality is applied only to actual feasible t.

Let ell<=min L and u>=max U be outward bounds. Set
 zl=max(-eta-10g,ell)/120,
 zu=min(eta+10g,u)/120,
 Z=max(|zl|,|zu|).
The actual z lies in[zl,zu]. Ordering must be checked. For a=(3-mu4)/24,
 a_star=max(|3-M|,|2-gl²|)/24.
This follows by enclosing mu4 in[1+gl²,M], not by assuming a>=0.

The correct c rectangle is
 cl=(3-M)*(gl if M<=3 else g)/144,
 cu=max_{t in[gl,g]}(2t-t³)/144.
For cu the maximum is at g if3g²<=2, at gl if3gl²>=2, otherwise at sqrt(2/3), where the value is sqrt(2/3)/108. These alternatives have exact squared-endpoint tests and require only an outward square-root enclosure in the third case. The lower endpoint comes from mu4<=M; the upper from mu4>=1+t². Either or both endpoints may be negative.

The exact Gaussian norm is J(z,c)=integral_0^infty exp(-x²/2)|zx²-cx⁴|dx. It is convex in the pair(z,c), so its maximum over the enclosing rectangle occurs at a corner. J(-z,-c)=J(z,c). For z*c<=0,
 J=sqrt(pi/2)(|z|+3|c|).
For z*c>0, simultaneously replace(z,c) by(|z|,|c|) and use the existing positive-quadrant Mills formula. This includes negative-negative corners; replacing only one sign would be invalid. The Gaussian coefficient multiplying z-3c needs the same sign-aware lower/upper enclosure already present in the retained implementation.

The sixth-moment upper remains
 M6=min(R*eta,1+3g²+g⁴+(R²+2Rg+3g²+2)*(M-1-gl²)).
All factors in the replacement are nonnegative. No R-2g gate occurs here. Substitute this M6, the new a_star, Z and the four signed J corner bounds into the unchanged transfer formulas for L0,LH,L2. Keep b0=(45-M)(1-2/n)/96; the old43/96 damping is not licensed. The Fourier transfer itself already covers M<=11.1,g<=1.85, regardless of coefficient signs.

Why changing only eligibility is invalid. Take the standardized two-point law X=3/2 with probability4/13 and X=-2/3 with probability9/13. Then gamma=5/6, mu4=61/36, and120z=1315/216. Valid loose bounds R=3/2,g=7/5,M=197/40 satisfy R>g and g²<2. The old quadratic maximum gives951/160, which is strictly smaller than1315/216. Thus it is not a valid bound on120|z| on the broadened domain. The signed piecewise construction above repairs this failed coefficient bound.

Implementation seam. Keep the same rmin across paired lower moment acquisition, eligibility, phase callback, all four C/rho corners and exports. For the first prototype retain0<pl<pu<1/2; extending to the other probability half is a distinct task. No new signed gamma reflection may enter the actual contact phase formula. All source/profile/moment inputs still need their own same-law acquisition and exact acceptance.

A4. WEIGHTED_LOSS_PROOF.txt
Original ordinary proof: work/finite5-source/logical-source-view-v2/work/cutoff5_20261020/entry/initial5_partition_v1/WEIGHTED_LOSS_PROOF.txt
Use in main: Physical resources: weighted constant-term loss for the same law
============================================================================

A quadratic signed-moment loss, with equality on every two-point law
===================================================================
Let X be centered, EX^2=1, with finite fifth absolute moment. Put
rho=E|X|^3, beta=E[X|X|], gamma=EX^3, m=E[X_+]=E[X_-]>0,
M4=EX^4, M5=E|X|^5 and delta=gamma-rho*beta.
Write Y=|X| and Z=Y^2-rho*Y. The exact identity
 V=E[Y Z^2]=M5-2rho*M4+rho^3
shows the nonnegative loss in the signed constant term:
 rho*M4/2-M5/4=rho^3/4-V/4.
Since delta=E[Y sign(X) Z], weighted Cauchy-Schwarz gives
 delta^2 <= E[Y] E[Y Z^2]=2m*V.
Consequently
 rho*M4/2-M5/4 <= rho^3/4 - (gamma-rho*beta)^2/(8m).       (1)
At X=0 the integrand and the weight vanish, so any convention for sign(0)
is harmless. Bounded support acquired in INITIAL supplies the fifth moment.
No physical defect D, gap, finite-n theorem, or optimizer is assumed here.

The bound is exact for every centered standardized two-point law: its
support is(-a,b), ab=1, and rho=(a^2+b^2)/(a+b). Then
 a^2-rho*a=-(b^2-rho*b), so Z is a constant times sign(X) on the support,
which is precisely equality in weighted Cauchy. Mixtures need not attain it.

Safe rectangular implementation
------------------------------
On rho in[rl,rh], beta in[bl,bu], gamma in[gl,gh], m<=mu, enclose
 delta in[min(g-r*b),max(g-r*b)] over all eight corners. Let dist be
its distance from0. Then dist^2/(8mu) is a lower bound for V/4 for EVERY
law in the box. It may be maximized with any previously established lower
loss. It must be subtracted only in the matching constant term, exactly
where the old quartic signed losses were subtracted. No upper bound on V
or lower bound on the physical gap is inferred from this argument.

Mathematical status: locally derived continuous inequality. Finite-law and
malformed-input controls are separate execution evidence, not a replacement
for the weighted Cauchy proof. A full INITIAL run is not asserted here.

A5. SIGN_PROBABILITY_PROOF.txt
Original ordinary proof: work/finite5-source/logical-source-view-v2/work/cutoff5_20261020/entry/initial5_partition_v1/SIGN_PROBABILITY_PROOF.txt
Use in main: C5/C6--9 INITIAL: sign-probability lower bound including a zero atom
============================================================================

A sharp sign-probability lower bound from the third absolute moment
=================================================================
Let EX=0,EX^2=1,rho=E|X|^3<infinity. Write p=P(X>0),
m=E[X_+]=E[X_-]>0, and a=E[X_+^2] in(0,1). Cauchy on each sign gives
 a^2<=m E[X_+^3], (1-a)^2<=m E[X_-^3], m^2<=p*a.
Consequently p*rho^2 >= (a^2+(1-a)^2)^2/a.
Set q(a)=2a^2-2a+1. The derivative of q(a)^2/a is
 q(a)*(6a^2-2a-1)/a^2. Since q>0, its unique minimum on(0,1)
is at a0=(1+sqrt(7))/6, with value
 kappa=(8/27)*(7sqrt(7)-17) = approximately .45044716.
Therefore P(X>0)>=kappa/rho^2 and, by reflection,
P(X<0)>=kappa/rho^2. The first statement does not require absence of a
zero atom. Once an actual central gap is acquired, the two probabilities
sum to1 and p belongs to[kappa/rho^2,1-kappa/rho^2]. Even without a gap,
P(X<0)<=1-p, so the same interval for p remains a valid necessary bound.

The constant is sharp: choose a=a0 and any0<p<1-a0, put m=sqrt(p*a0),
and give a/m positive mass p, -(1-a)/m negative mass p*a/(1-a), with the
remaining probability at0. These laws are centered, unit variance, and
have p*rho^2=kappa. This equality example is explanatory; the exact
implementation only uses the continuous Cauchy and derivative proof.

Implementation uses rational kappa0=11261/25000=.45044 < kappa, witnessed
by sqrt7>10583/4000=2.64575 and explicit rational substitution. Every
rho<=rh therefore satisfies p>=kappa0/rh^2 and p<=1-kappa0/rh^2.
No n, support bound, physicalD, phase, or optimizer is used.

A6. OUTER_RESOURCE_PROOF.txt
Original ordinary proof: work/finite5-source/logical-source-view-v2/work/cutoff5_20261020/entry/initial5_partition_v1/OUTER_RESOURCE_PROOF.txt
Use in main: Resource-to-COUNT: outer-gap prices spend one residual
============================================================================

A bounded-support refinement of the outer central-gap budget
===========================================================
Conditional on the same-law genuine central gap(-A,B), signed support radii
Rplus,Rminus, and D<=d, all as separately acquired by the current stage.
Write m=E[X_+]=E[X_-], R+=E[X_+(X_+-B)], and similarly R-.
Let T+=E[X_+^3]-mB^2 and T-=E[X_-^3]-mA^2. Then pointwise on the positive
support X>=B,
 X(X^2-B^2)=(X+B)X(X-B)<=(Rplus+B)X(X-B).
Thus T+<=(Rplus+B)R+, and the reflected inequality gives
T-<=(Rminus+A)R-. Both T,R are nonnegative.

The exact gap decomposition is
 D=mQ+(sigma-1)T++(sigma+1)T-+3(T-Jo),
 T-Jo>=2BR++2AR-, sigma=sqrt(10),
 Q=(sqrt(sigma+1)A-sqrt(sigma-1)B)^2.
Consequently D-mQ>=wp_actual*T++wm_actual*T-, where
 wp_actual=sigma-1+6B/(Rplus+B),
 wm_actual=sigma+1+6A/(Rminus+A).
For an acquired rectangle A>=Al>0,B>=Bl>0, the safe rational weights are
 wp=SL-1+6Bl/(Rplus+Bl), wm=SL+1+6Al/(Rminus+Al).
The ratios increase in the positive endpoint and decrease in the radius.
Both weights are positive, and wp*T++wm*T-<=D-mQ.

Thus with qm<=Q, dout=d-ml*qm>=0,
 T+<=min(Bplus_upper-ml*Bl^2,dout/wp),
 T-<=min(Bminus_upper-ml*Al^2,dout/wm).
The positive-part versions in code are safe because actual T+ and T- are
nonnegative. These replace only the corresponding older outer resource
bounds. The existing integral inequalities remain valid, giving
 outer<=(3/8)max(kp/wp,km/wm)*(D-mQ),
 kp=(rho_hi-Bl)_+^2, km=(Rminus-rho_lo)_+^2.
This supplies lambda for the separately proved central physical-D price;
its actual D is retained, not replaced by a copy before rearrangement.
The signed U interval uses the same improved T+ and T- upper bounds.

All other positivity checks, unseeded first stage, signed-gap acquisition,
actual score intervals, and complete candidate maxima remain unchanged.
No bound applies to a nongap cell. No count-domain extension is asserted.

A7. LOWER_GAP_PROOF.txt
Original ordinary proof: work/finite5-source/logical-source-view-v2/work/cutoff5_20261020/entry/initial5_partition_v1/LOWER_GAP_PROOF.txt
Use in main: Positive-gap acquisition: localizing-matrix endpoint/sign contraction
============================================================================

Lower central-gap localizing matrices and common-mean contraction
================================================================
Assume a genuine central gap(-A,B), with proved positive lower endpoints
Al<=A,Bl<=B. On positive support X>=Bl. Under the measure weighted by
(X-Bl)1_{X>0}, Cauchy gives
 (m-Bl*p)(B3-Bl*A2)>=(A2-Bl*m)^2,
 A2=E X_+^2,B3=E X_+^3,m=E X_+,p=P(X>0).
Both factors and A2-Bl*m are nonnegative. Given m<=mu,A2>=a2lo,
B3<=b3hi, write den=b3hi-Bl*a2lo. Then den bounds the second factor
above, and (a2lo-Bl*mu)_+ bounds A2-Bl*m below. Consequently
 p<=[mu-(a2lo-Bl*mu)_+^2/den]/Bl if den>0.
If den<0 the cell is impossible. If den=0, actual second factor is zero;
a positive lower numerator excludes the cell, otherwise p<=mu/Bl remains
valid. Negative output likewise excludes any positive-probability law.
Reflection supplies the corresponding upper bound on q=1-p.

For fixed moment/endpoints, P_+(m),P_-(m) defined by these formulas are
nondecreasing in m. For den>0 their slope is
 [1+2Bl*(a2lo-Bl*m)_+/den]/Bl>0; the zero case is a monotone feasible ray.
Thus the necessary conditions P_+>=pl,P_->=1-pu,P_++P_->=1 are monotone
in m. Failure at mu excludes the whole cell. Otherwise finite exact
bisection keeps an infeasible lower endpoint and feasible upper endpoint;
returning the LOWER endpoint is a valid lower bound on every feasible m.
There is no convergence assumption. The function also intersects p using
caps at mu. Existing broader interval enclosures remain valid when m and
p are contracted. All endpoint/radius inputs must be separately acquired;
this helper cannot prove the existence of a central gap.

A8. EXACT_PHASE_PROOF.txt
Original ordinary proof: work/finite5-source/logical-source-view-v2/work/cutoff5_20261020/entry/initial5_partition_v1/EXACT_PHASE_PROOF.txt
Use in main: C5/C6--9/C10--19: exact Gaussian phase on acquired H interval
============================================================================

EXACT GAUSSIAN PHASE AND AFFINE DEFECT PRICING
Locally derived continuous reduction, 2026-10-18. Numerical controls/review
are separate. No new physical-gap or contact identity is assumed proved here.

1. Explicit input contract
Fix n in{6,7,8,9}, one positive-branch standardized optimizing witness,
and a raw sum threshold H whose absolute value is ALREADY bounded by Hmax.
Let C be its normalized score, rho=E|X|^3, gamma=EX^3, and D>=0 the
physical gap defect. Write phi=phi(H/sqrt(n)), phi0=phi(0),
CE=(3+sqrt(10))*phi0/6. The retained conserved contact/Fourier identity
and same-law coefficient estimates must give
 n phi D/6 + n(C-CE exp(-H^2/(2n)))*rho
 <= KF+slope*|H|+C*Aactual+H*(C*K+phi*U)
                      -H^2*(C*rho+gamma*phi/2).      (1)
Here KF,slope are nonnegative COMPLETE Fourier costs; all omitted conserved
slack terms must have the nonnegative sign needed for this upper bound.
The helper itself does not prove (1), acquire Hmax, or acquire moments.
Assume the same law satisfies independently acquired intervals
 C in[cl,cu], rho in[rl,rh], gamma>=gl, K in[kl,ku], U in[ul,uu],
and the upper affine coefficient bound
 Aactual<=Astar-price*D, price>=0.                    (2)
Taking price=0 is the old A upper bound and needs no new defect-price lemma.
The code requires positive score, rho>=1, and all stated interval gates.

2. Keep the Gaussian phase exactly
For H=sign*u, u>=0, choose K=ku,U=uu for sign=+1 and K=kl,U=ul for
sign=-1. The sign choices maximize the two H terms. Replace gamma by its
lower endpoint in the negative square term. Substituting(2), moving its
C*price*D to the left, and using CE/phi0=(3+sqrt(10))/6, gives
 D <= [P(u)+phi Q(u)]/[n*phi/6+C*price],              (3)
 P(u)=KF+C*Astar-n*C*rho+(slope+sign*C*K)u-C*rho*u^2,
 Q(u)=n*(3+sqrt(10))*rho/6+sign*U*u-gl*u^2/2.
The denominator is strictly positive. In particular this argument never
replaces phi by an unproved raw-density floor and never divides an upper
bound by a possibly negative coefficient.

3. Complete C,rho reduction
For fixed u and actual phi, the right side of(3) is affine in rho with a
positive denominator independent of rho. Its maximum occurs at rl or rh.
For fixed rho,u, it is a fractional-affine function of C with a positive
affine denominator. The derivative has constant sign, or is identically
zero. Therefore its maximum occurs at cl or cu. Thus all four corners
suffice, even though C and rho are not independent characteristics of an
actual law. The rectangle only enlarges the feasible set.
This does not invoke a convexity claim for an optimizer or iid laws.

4. Every H cell, both signs, all endpoints
Choose mesh m and L=ceil(m Hmax)/m. Cover[0,L] by all consecutive CLOSED
cells [j/m,(j+1)/m]. This enlarges the previously acquired H range, so it
cannot lose a witness. Both signs are evaluated. There is no infinite-H
tail obligation here precisely because Hmax is an explicit input theorem;
without that input this helper has no valid global scope.
On each cell maximize P and Q separately, exactly: a quadratic has its
maximum at an endpoint or its interior stationary point if its leading
coefficient is negative. These maxima Pmax,Qmax are rational once the
upper sqrt(10) is chosen in Q's positive constant. For every point of the
cell, phi lies in[phi_lower(right),phi_upper(left)] by monotonicity on
u>=0. Replacing P,Q by Pmax,Qmax increases the numerator, with positive
denominator. The resulting expression
 (Pmax+phi*Qmax)/(n*phi/6+C*price)
is again fractional-affine in phi, so its maximum is at one of these two
positive endpoints. The largest value over the complete finite inventory
is the output D_upper. Negative output is a contradiction to D>=0, not an
invented positive defect value; handling that contradiction belongs to the
caller. No cell is sampled as a substitute for a continuous bound.

5. Arithmetic and allowed integration
The standalone rational Gaussian kernel is copied byte-for-byte from the
independently reviewed HIGH6 epoch. It bounds pi, square roots and exp(-x)
outward. Densities use(2*pi)^(-1/2)*exp(-u^2/(2n)), with closed point
arguments and the same exact interval stack. The rounded H domain must
satisfy L^2/(2n)<=7; failure is unresolved and cannot be ignored.
All subsequent arithmetic is exact Fraction arithmetic. Domain and source
checks do not acquire(1),(2),Hmax or any support condition. A stage may take
the minimum of this complete bound and another complete bound for the
same D, but may not insert a moment-only defect in place of physical D.
The positive-density stack and shared-law coefficient acquisition remain
separately documented dependencies of this new helper.

A9. CENTRAL_PRICE_PROOF.txt
Original ordinary proof: work/finite5-source/logical-source-view-v2/work/cutoff5_20261020/entry/initial5_partition_v1/CENTRAL_PRICE_PROOF.txt
Use in main: Conserved resource: central signed price and positive total denominator
============================================================================

Retain the same physical D in the acquired central-gap envelope
==============================================================
This is a conditional refinement after a genuine same-law central gap has
already been acquired. It does not acquire the gap or an initial D seed.
Let rho,beta,gamma,J,D and sigma=sqrt(10) have the retained meanings.
Write Q=(sqrt(sigma+1)A-sqrt(sigma-1)B)^2 for the central asymmetry.
Suppose a valid common moment/endpoint cell provides

 Aactual <= (3/2)beta(J+gamma) + central + outer
            +rho^3/4-rho/2-penalty,
 outer <= lambda*(D-mQ),   mQ>=mq_lower>=0,

where central,lambda,penalty are valid nonnegative upper/lower quantities
in the directions indicated. In the present source,
 central=3*mu*max(G(Au,rho_l),G(Au,rho_u)),
 lambda=(3/8)*max((rho_u-Bl)_+^2/(SL-1),(Rminus-rho_l)_+^2/(SL+1)),
 mq_lower=ml*qm,
and the acquired central decomposition supplies D-mQ>=0. Since lambda
already encloses the whole coefficient of that nonnegative outer budget,
outer<=lambda*D-lambda*mq_lower. No independent copy of D is introduced.

Substitute the exact relation J=((3+sigma)rho-gamma-D)/3 only in the
beta term. Then

 Aactual <= Acenter_star - tcenter*D,
 tcenter=beta/2-lambda,
 Acenter_star=beta*((3+sigma)*rho/2+gamma)+central-lambda*mq_lower
              +rho^3/4-rho/2-penalty.

A safe closed-cell upper constant is the maximum of the first multiaffine
term over every rho,beta,gamma,sigma endpoint, plus the fixed central term,
minus lambda*mq_lower, plus rho_u^3/4-rho_l/2-penalty. The price lower
endpoint is beta_lower/2-lambda. Since D>=0, these give a valid affine
upper bound for the SAME Aactual. It may be intersected with the earlier
coarse and unpriced bounds only AFTER each supplies its complete maximum
over the actual score interval. The positive total-denominator check in
shared_gap_price.defect_upper remains mandatory even if tcenter is negative.

The same convex-numerator/positive-affine-denominator score argument applies
unchanged: all cell constants and price are independent of score C. The
already acquired raw sign license, complete LOW/HIGH, phase curvature and
score-charge breakpoints are unchanged. In cells without an acquired central
gap this candidate is unavailable; the earlier candidates remain in force.

A10. PROOF.txt
Original ordinary proof: work/low2630-support-v1/actual59a/task/capsule/work/cutoff25_continue_20261015/analytic/bernoulli_eta_zeta_v1/PROOF.txt
Use in main: C26--39 B1: conditional eta/zeta with full moment/support hypotheses
============================================================================

A Bernoulli-shaped scalar envelope for the B1 symmetrization parameters
=====================================================================
Mathematical status: locally derived elementary theorem, conditional on the
same-law sign/support resources below. No Fourier or cutoff claim is made.

Theorem. In the actual sign-coordinate notation of fixed_h_eta_zeta_v1,
assume8/25<=p<=43/100,0<=s<=13/1000, and1.1<=rho<=1.2. Suppose the joint
inward allowance is at most a fixed D with0<D<=9/100. Let fixed C,Q give
 rho<=U(v,s)=[v(1-2v)+Cs-s^2/Q]/(v+s)^(3/2), v=p(1-p),
where2<=C<=3,Q>=2/5. Assume
  C+(75/16)D<3,                                        (A)
  8pl-5pl^2-2-(1-pl)D>=0 for a valid pl<=p.            (B)
Then, with beta(r)=(sqrt(r^2+8)-r)/2,
              eta<=beta(rho)<=zeta.                   (T)
Here eta=E|X-X'|^3/2-rho and zeta=4E(X_+)^2 E(X_-)^2/E|X|.
All55 original B1 resources are checked against these explicit gates.

1. Elementary starting inequalities
------------------------------------
The independent proofs in fixed_h_eta_zeta_v1 and firstmean_envelope_v2 give
 eta<=E(p,s,D):=2[v^2+D(1-p)s]/(v+s)^(3/2),
 zeta>=Z(p,s):=2(v^2+ps)/(v+s)^(3/2),
 rho>=I(p,s,D):=[v(1-2v)+(3p-D)s+s^2/((1-p)D)]/(v+s)^(3/2).
For I, the centered one-sided cubic inequality EU^3<=a EU^2-(EU^2)^2/a
on U<=a, followed by b^2/(qa0)+c^2/(pa1)>=s^2/(qD), supplies the
quadratic term; zero inward caps force their conditional variance zero.
The outward upper U uses the same cubic inequality on the reflected
conditional laws, then b^2/(qL0)+c^2/(pL1)>=s^2/Q; its C,Q are uniform
upper coefficients from the same acquired resource, as in fixed_h_outward_v1.
The eta upper increases with D, so an acquired upper allowance may replace
the actual inward sum. These formulas cover s=0. If the actual inward sum
vanishes, the law is Bernoulli; the positive chosen D still gives valid
inequalities. No variance allocation or noise grid is introduced here.

2. Zeta side: an exact polynomial identity
------------------------------------------
Put w=v+s,M=v^2+ps and let N be the numerator of I. Direct expansion gives
 (I Z+Z^2-2)w^3/2 = M(N+2M)-w^3
 =s*v*p*[8p-5p^2-2-(1-p)D]
  +s^2*p*[pq/D+8p-3-D]
  +s^3*[p/(qD)-1].                                   (1)
The first coefficient is nonnegative by(B), since its bracket increases
in p (derivative8-10p+D>0). The second bracket is at least
(1/5)/(9/100)+8*(8/25)-3-9/100=1523/900>0.
The last bracket is at least647/153>0. Hence I Z+Z^2>=2.
Since rho>=I, zeta>=Z>0, it follows that rho*zeta+zeta^2>=2.
The positive root of x^2+rho*x-2=0 is beta(rho), proving zeta>=beta(rho).
Equality holds at s=0. The polynomial inequality alone uses no upper noise
bound and no outward cap.

3. Eta side: monotonicity along a third-moment upper level
---------------------------------------------------------
Write a=Dq, w=v+s, M=v^2+a s and N=v-2v^2+Cs-s^2/Q. Define
 Js=(C-2s/Q)w-(3/2)N
   =v(C-3/2+3v)-(C/2+2v/Q)s-s^2/(2Q),
 Jv=(1-4v)w-(3/2)N
   =-v/2-v^2+s(1-4v-3C/2)+3s^2/(2Q).
On1/5<=v<=1/4,0<=s<=1/40,2<=C<=3,Q>=2/5,
 Js>=963/6400>0 and Jv<=-881/6400<0. Thus U increases in s and decreases
in v throughout this enlarged rectangle. Also E decreases in s, since
 its s-derivative numerator/2 is
 Ks=av-(3/2)v^2-as/2<0,
using a/v=D/p<=9/32<3/10.

For a fixed value a, the v-derivative numerator of E/2 is
 Kv=v^2/2+(2v-3a/2)s. The determinant Kv*Js-Ks*Jv has coefficients
 T0=(v^2/2)[v(C-3)+a(1+2v)],
 T1=-(C/2+3/2)v^2+av(1-v)-v^3/Q,
 T2=-Cv+a(1/2-2v)+(-2v^2+3av/2)/Q,
 T3=(3a/2-v)/Q,                                      (2)
for powers1,s,s^2,s^3 respectively (T0 is the constant coefficient).
All four are strictly negative: T0 uses(A), a/v=D/p<=D/(8/25) and
1+2v<=3/2; T1 uses a/v<3/10 and C>=2; T2 uses1/2-2v<=1/10,
a/v<3/10,C>=2 and -2v^2+3av/2<0; T3 uses a/v<3/10.
For the actual p derivative, v'=r=1-2p and a'=-D. Its determinant becomes
 r*(T0+T1s+T2s^2+T3s^3)-D*s*w*Js<0                  (3)
when p<1/2. Therefore E decreases strictly in p along any level U=R
inside this rectangle. The factors omitted from the derivative are positive.

4. Moving to the Bernoulli point is legitimate
----------------------------------------------
Fix R=rho in[1.1,1.2]. There is a unique p_B in(0,1/2) with
 rho_B(p_B)=(1-2p_B(1-p_B))/sqrt(p_B(1-p_B))=R.
Because rho_B decreases, rho_B(8/25)^2=124609/85000>(6/5)^2, we have
p_B>8/25. Let v_B=p_B(1-p_B), so2sqrt(v_B)=beta(R).
If actual p<=p_B, then eta<=E|X|<=2sqrt(v)<=beta(R), immediately.
Otherwise p_B<actual p<=43/100. Since U(actual v,actual s)>=R and U_s>0,
there is a unique s_R at actual v with U=R and0<=s_R<=actual s. For EVERY
v between v_B and actual v, U(v,actual s)>=U(actual v,actual s)>=R,
whereas U(v,0)<=R. Thus the continuous level root exists throughout that
interval and stays in[0,actual s] subset[0,13/1000]. The implicit derivative
is well-defined because Js>0. This supplies the entire path required by(3),
not merely its endpoints. Since E decreases in s and along this path in p,
 eta<=E(actual p,actual s,D)<=E(actual p,s_R,D)
       <=E(p_B,0,D)=2sqrt(v_B)=beta(R).
The constants C,Q,D remain FIXED along the virtual path; they are algebraic
majorant parameters, not claims of acquired support resources at virtual p.
This completes(T).

5. Exact scope and reusable interval form
-----------------------------------------
For a closed rho interval[Rlo,Rhi] contained in[1.1,1.2], beta decreases,
so the theorem gives eta<=beta(Rlo) and zeta>=beta(Rhi). These endpoints
must not be interchanged. The checker supplies rational upper/lower brackets
for beta at fixed hundredth endpoints, verifies their quadratic signs, checks
all55 resource gates(A),(B), and checks the exact polynomial/derivative
identities on adversarial rational fixtures. Continuous validity follows
from the proofs above, not from the finite fixture count.

The supporting resource-acquisition theorem and the independent sine-circle
bridge from zeta to Im f remain explicit premises in applications. Arithmetic
PASS does not prove any Fourier integral or remove an inventory panel.

A11. PROOF.txt
Original ordinary proof: work/low2630-support-v1/actual59a/task/capsule/work/cutoff25_continue_20261015/analytic/fixed_h_eta_zeta_v1/PROOF.txt
Use in main: Fourier/resource: fixed common-frequency eta/zeta envelope
============================================================================

Eta and zeta envelopes from a single first-absolute-moment interval
================================================================
Mathematical status: new elementary reparameterization and endpoint bounds.
Execution status: prepared exact map; receipt records actual execution.
No Fourier integrations or panel exclusions are contained in this unit.

1. Coordinates and first moment
-------------------------------
Let 0<p<=1/2, q=1-p, v=pq. In the actual sign coordinates Y=-p+U0 on
mass q and Y=q+U1 on mass p, E Ui=0. Put b=q E U0^2,c=p E U1^2,
s=b+c,w=v+s,X=Y/sqrt(w),h=E|X|=2v/sqrt(w),rho=E|X|^3.
Assume inward endpoints U0<=a0, -U1<=a1 with a0+a1<=d<=p.
For an independent X' with the same marginal, define
 eta=E|X-X'|^3/2-rho.
Define zeta=4 A2 B2/h, A2=E(X_+)^2,B2=E(X_-)^2. A separate established
sine-circle theorem gives |Im f(u)|<=sqrt(rho^2-zeta^2)|u|^3/6. Its source
is bound as a premise; this unit independently derives the needed zeta lower.
The eta bound enters the symmetrized third moment rho+eta.

2. Eta: exact deficit and fixed-h formula
-----------------------------------------
The scalar identity
 |x-y|^3=(x-y)^2(|x|+|y|)-2 min(|x|,|y|)(x-y)^2 1_{xy>0}
gives eta=h minus the expectation of its last factor without the2.
In each same-sign conditional pair, the magnitude is at least(p-a0)/sqrt(w)
or(q-a1)/sqrt(w), and the expected difference square is2 Var Ui/w.
Thus the deficit is at least
 2[q b(p-a0)+p c(q-a1)]/w^(3/2)
 >=2s(v-qd)/w^(3/2).
At s=0 this is still valid and eta=h. At a sign boundary zero, the relevant
lower magnitude can vanish; the inequality still holds. Therefore
 eta<=2(v^2+s qd)/(v+s)^(3/2)
     =E(p,h,d):=h^3/(4v)*(1-d/p)+(d/p)h.              (1)
This follows by substituting s=4v^2/h^2-v. No independent denominator or
noise endpoint substitution occurs.
For fixed p,d with0<=d<=p, E strictly increases with h>0. For fixed h,
set a=h^2/(4v). On a<=1,
 (1/h) E_p= -a(1-2p)/v*(1-d/p) - d/p^2*(1-a)<=0.     (2)
For fixed p,h with a<=1, E increases in d. Consequently if an actual-law
box has pl<=p<=pu and an established h<=H with H^2<=4vl, vl=pl(1-pl),
then eta<=E(pl,H,dhi) for any valid uniform inward allowance dhi<=pl.
If H^2>4vl, use the already valid eta<=h<=H instead. Never use (2) across
the inadmissible a>1 domain to infer an improved eta upper bound.

3. Tightening the inward allowance using H alone
------------------------------------------------
For0<H<=1 and vl>=1/5, the function4v^2/H^2-v increases for v>=vl.
Thus h<=H gives the same-law lower noise bound
  s>=sL:=max(0,4vl^2/H^2-vl).
If sL>sh, the box is empty. Otherwise any acquired nonincreasing affine
inward upper budget d(s)<=min(A-Bs,C-Ds), with B,D>=0, gives
 dhi=min(A-B sL,C-D sL).
A negative dhi means incompatibility, not permission to replace it by zero.
The B1 map checks0<=dhi<=pl before using it. At dhi=0 the actual conditional
noises vanish, but the upper bound in(1) remains valid. This refinement
requires no extra p/s partition.

4. Zeta: concavity replaces allocation and noise grids
------------------------------------------------------
The signwise second moments are A2=(vq+c)/w and B2=(vp+b)/w. Therefore
 zeta=2(vp+b)(vq+c)/(v w^(3/2)).
For b=s(1-alpha),c=s alpha, the numerator product is concave in alpha.
Its minimum on[0,1] is the smaller endpoint, namely alpha=1 when p<=q.
This yields, for EVERY s>=0, without the gate s<=v(q-p),
 zeta>=2(v^2+ps)/(v+s)^(3/2)
      =Z(p,h):=h/q-h^3/(4q^2).                       (3)
Moreover zeta>=h follows directly from
 (vp+b)(vq+c)-v^2(v+s)=v(p^2c+q^2b)+bc>=0.
For p<=43/100 and0<h<=1,
 Z_p=h(q-h^2/2)/q^3>0,
 Z_hh=-3h/(2q^2)<0.
Hence if L<=h<=H<=1 and pl<=p<=pu<=43/100, a safe bound is
 zeta>=max(L,min(Z(pl,L),Z(pl,H))).                  (4)
This uses concavity on the entire h interval, not sampled minima. It works
also on boxes where Z changes monotonicity. When L^2>=4(1-pl)/3, Z decreases
in h throughout and the minimum in(4) is simply Z(pl,H).
Choose L as a rational downward rounding of2vl/sqrt(vl+sh), and take H
from the prior fixed-h outward map. All expressions in(1)--(4) are rational
for the supplied L,H and acquired rational coefficients.

5. Receipt scope and checks
---------------------------
The exact map takes every495 nonempty rho-case in the frozen fixed-h map,
re-reads the same55 original actual-law resources, computes sL,dhi,eta_upper,
and zeta_lower, and checks all stated gates and index identities. The old
first-moment cap H is retained in a separate field: eta has a different
meaning here. The values hold for all actual rho at least the case's R and
at most its acquired upper endpoint.
Finite controls compare both reparameterizations to their raw p/s forms,
check exact derivative identities and all-alpha endpoint minima, zero noise,
Bernoulli equality, malformed input and corrupted/missing/duplicate/reordered
objects. They supplement the continuous arguments above. The acquired support
and sine-circle premises remain explicit. No actual-law existence or complete
Berry--Esseen claim is inferred from surviving parameter tuples.

A12. EXACT_SINE_ADAPTER_PROOF.txt
Original ordinary proof: work/low2630-support-v1/actual59a/task/capsule/work/cutoff25_continue_20261015/phase_rectangle_v1/EXACT_SINE_ADAPTER_PROOF.txt
Use in main: C26--39 B1 interface: exact sine-strip adapter
============================================================================

Exact all55-resource map and23-strip sine schedule
================================================
This adapter establishes exact arithmetic premises and domain coverage. It
does not evaluate any Fourier integral and cannot establish cutoff25.
It imports only standard library and the previously reviewed exact parser.

1. Original acquisition and the conditional eta envelope
The parser requires the unique B1 band[11/10,6/5], literal n26..30 and
index32..42, matching integer inner N/index and exact hundredth p intervals.
For each of55 rows, the adapter independently recomputes
 sh=min(lambda_max,acquired_lambda_upper)/n,
 D=min(d0,(budgetD-charge_b)/charge_g),
 C=max(3pu+outer0,3(1-pl)+outer1),
 Q=max((1-pl)outer0+pl outer1,(1-pu)outer0+pu outer1).
It verifies every hypothesis of the conditional eta<=beta(rho) theorem:
pl>=8/25,pu<=43/100,sh<=13/1000,0<D<=9/100,2<=C<=3,Q>=2/5,
C+75D/16<3, and the original theorem's zeta polynomial gate. The values and
margins must agree with the independently checked frozen55-row beta receipt.
The unconditional proof of zeta>=beta(rho) is separately bound as a premise.

2. Independent finite-u support map
Joint inward support a0+a1<=D, with D<=q-p and D<p, implies
 (p-a0)^2+(q-a1)^2>=p^2+(q-D)^2.
The right side decreases in p throughout the acquired interval; after
standardizing, its lower bound is[pu^2+(1-pu-D)^2]/[pu(1-pu)+sh].
The adapter checks this is>=17/10, and checks the squared sum of the actual
standardized radii<=1037/100 and each radius<=13/5. These are same-law support
premises for the finite-u sine-circle theorem. No sampled support minimum is
accepted. The parent's independently constructed map is bound as an input,
but this adapter recomputes all55 support inequalities from the acquisition.

3. Rational uniform rho inputs
Each of23 strips has exact closed endpoints, starting at11/10 and ending at6/5
with matching seams. They over-enclose every original row's rho interval.
For hi, rational zl,zu must obey
 0<zl<=zu, zl^2+hi*zl<=2<=zu^2+hi*zu.
Thus zl<=beta(hi)<=zu. Since beta decreases and rho+beta(rho) increases,
the valid simultaneous inputs are
 rho+eta<=hi+zu=:D_upper,
 rho^2-zeta^2<=hi^2-zl^2.
In particular zu is NOT asserted to bound eta uniformly over the strip.
The adapter requires the proposed D_upper and zeta_lower to match exactly.
The Fourier integrator may use R(u)=1-(17/10)u^2/20*(1-(1037/100)u^2/42)
only under u*(13/5)<=pi and (1037/100)u^2<=42, retaining the old circle
outside. Fourth-moment and corner bounds have their separate explicit gates.
All115 integrals (23 strips times five n) remain independent obligations.

4. Structural controls and trusted stack
Missing/duplicated strips, gaps, overlaps, wrong n, wrong D/zeta/beta bracket,
altered A/B/M or kappa, missing original resources, wrong raw N, invalid
support radii, negative inward caps and corrupted gate receipts must reject.
Reordering strips is accepted. Proposed floating quadrature values are ignored.
Only their declared n and quadrature-order keys are checked for scope mistakes.
Arithmetic uses Python integers/Fraction. The exact integral target4097/10000
is recorded as an obligation, not a conclusion.

All acquired B1 resources: a one-parameter scalar Fourier cover

A14. UNIFORM25_CANTELLI_PRODUCTGAP_REVIEW.txt
Original ordinary proof: work/n25-mixed-current28-source-v1/n25-first28-source-cache/cutoff25_20261011/continuation_20261010/audit35/UNIFORM25_CANTELLI_PRODUCTGAP_REVIEW.txt
Use in main: Weighted floor25: Cantelli/product-gap transport to actual index
============================================================================

Conditional floor-25 extension of Cantelli/product-gap INITIAL and finite feedback
2026-10-10. Source/mathematical review only; no new cutoff, exact acquisition,
actual-index support proof or count acceptance is asserted by this note.

Assessment
----------
For the intended actual indices m=26,...,30 and sharp contrary target K=0,
the new formulas introduce no additional obstruction to the previously
reviewed floor-N induction, with N=25. The stronger invariant remains

  raw_actual <= raw_saved,  m*D_actual <= 25*d_saved,
  H_actual = sqrt(m)*z_actual.

Keep all geometric/moment quantities as same-law physical quantities, and
keep every stored Fourier coefficient and phase polynomial evaluated at25.
This is a theorem about a saved exact sequence; it is NOT a claim that a
fresh adaptively rounded computation at m returns a smaller dictionary.
The source candidate has a 20<=n<=40 gate. This note's intended composition
scope is25<=m<=40 (in particular26..30), not an unqualified executable
all-index claim. An extension beyond40 would need its own analytic scope.

Source families and status
--------------------------
Q = toward25/quarter2_25_coarsened_exact/
P = toward25/quarter3_resource_feedback_probe/
R = toward25/float_resource25_productgap/
All paths are relative to continuation_20261010. Their exact bytes and the
prior floor31 review notes are bound by the companion review JSON.

Q/initial_q2.py:13 fixes n=nmax=25,K=0; :15-19 installs identical integer
exponential arithmetic, typed caching, Cantelli HIGH and upward M coarsening.
Its completed exact INITIAL/resource replay is an available floor25 input.
Q/initial34.py has the older gap distance (line100), not the new product-gap
strengthening. The current negative25_exact/initial34.py is also the older
formula. Therefore do not relabel Q2 as a product-gap computation.

The completed Q2 RESOURCE stage does use the branch-aware endpoint-product
price P=(SL-1)*rho_lo-d in resource25_core.py:128-130, along with the actual
positive-gap initialization. However, its contracted_kernel.py:262-266
uses only max(A+B,sqrt(2*rho_lo-2*d/(SL-1))) for the HIGH distance. It does
NOT use the newer signed-gamma price or HIGH square2*P/(3*mu). Thus Q2
contains Cantelli and branch-aware endpoint-product refinements, while its
INITIAL remains gap-only and its HIGH remains the older gap distance.
The prospective extension must retain each frozen variant exactly.

P/initial34_product_gap.py is the new, separately reviewed signed-price/
product-gap formula, presently used by named FLOAT discovery. R contains
the corresponding resource strengthening and finite feedback drivers.
A future exact capsule with these formulas must acquire/replay its own
arithmetic before this uniform interpretation can consume it.

New formula checks
------------------
1. Cantelli HIGH (Q/cantelli_high.py and shared/high_event_probe proof):
   V=2M+2,h=2-delta^2,a=h^2/(V+h^2),mevent=2+V/h depend only on the retained
   same-law M bound and chosen fixed delta. Their event theorem has no n.
   Every optional candidate retains all HIGH_WINDOW50 six terms and gates
   2*N*c>1. Thus one complete selected candidate at N remains an upper
   bound at all m>=N, exactly as the old HIGH proof. Optional candidates
   invalid at N need not be added at m. Neither a changing minimizer nor
   numerical monotonicity of the rounded exphi routine is needed.

2. Q/high_grid.py replaces M by U=ceil(1000*M)/1000, unless this would exceed
   the original domain. U is a valid fourth-moment cap and independent of n.
   Fix that U throughout the uniform interpretation. Typed caches and the
   integer polynomial evaluator change evaluation order only. Q/high_window_
   core.py caches fixed constants, without changing the analytic HIGH terms.

3. P/initial34_product_gap.py:94-127 and R/candidate_charged_score_v2.py:33-60
   use only D_actual<=d_saved and retained same-law rho, beta, gamma, mean,
   theta and physical endpoint/support bounds. In particular
     P=max((SL-1)*rho_lo, SL*rho_lo-gamma_hi)-d_saved
   is a conservative positive endpoint-product price and
     gap^2 >= max(2*rho_lo-2*d_saved/(SL-1), 2*P/(3*mu))
   is a valid resource HIGH distance. INITIAL also has the separate valid
   mean-based distance (rho_lo-d_saved/(SL-1))/mu. None introduces n.
   Retain the positive-gap, positive-mean and HIGH distance<=12 gates.
   The false beta*rho inference is absent from these formulas.

4. R/general_paired_exact.py:38-51 gives index-free eligibility and moment
   coefficients, including both signs of its signed Pearson rectangle.
   Lines57-74 contain all n dependence: fac=(n/(n-1))^(3/2), positive
   fac/(n-1) terms, and positive remainders divided by powers of
   a0=(n-1)/(2n), b0=(45-M)*(1-2/n)/96. The numerator factors are nonnegative
   under the retained gates; the whole LOW coefficients decrease. The
   further l2/sqrt(n) decreases too. Keep its p<1/2 gate; the general
   unpaired route remains the fallback on other probability panels.

5. The new D>2 allowance does not spoil density/curvature monotonicity.
   For R/contracted_kernel.py:284 the derivative in phi is
     [3.16*rho-3-d+max(d-.16,0)]/2 >= 0 for rho>=1,d>=0.
   For d>=.16 it equals3.16*(rho-1)/2; otherwise it is at least(.16-d)/2.
   Replacing actual D by the weaker saved d only decreases that curvature.
   The corrected paired callback explicitly uses
     C*rho + min(phi_N*g0,.4*g0)/2, g0=3.16*rho-3-d.
   It remains conservative whether g0 is positive or negative. Retain the
   positivity gates on every actual saved call and the positive rebate
   (.4095*rho-d/15)*(1-2.5*raw/N)/2.

6. P/initial34_product_gap.py:143-159 has precisely the old floor-index
   density, own-score and charged-budget structure. With K=0 the score
   floor is constant .4095. The fixed density interval at25 contains the
   actual interval at m. The own-score numerator at25 is larger than at m.
   The actual excess charge m*(C-CE)*rho is at least
   25*max(0,C-.40974)*rho. The named breakpoint .40974 preserves the convex
   score-corner reduction. Multiplication by15000/(997*25) exports a bound
   on (m/25)*D_actual, preserving the weighted invariant.
   None of these statements automatically transfers a positive K/n target
   or a stored positive-K score floor to a sharp target at later m.

7. Finite feedback is a finite induction on the same attained unrestricted
   global witness, not a new constrained maximum. Once the earlier INITIAL
   and resource sequence has the uniform interpretation, intersect its
   rho/M/eta/gamma/mean/beta bounds with the next fixed moment cover. Retain
   literal p and rho subbranches, all lower/upper directions, and the same
   physical support rays. Using min on saved d values preserves mD<=25d;
   upward padding preserves it too. A later cover may use the weaker
   D_actual<=d_saved in all moment geometry. The next charged budget again
   restores the weighted invariant. No limit or eventual convergence is
   invoked. All feedback edges must bind both incoming source states.

8. Every stored H cap remains a cap on H_actual=sqrt(m)*z, since the floor25
   phase polynomial is valid pointwise in that H. A scalar resource fallback
   in Q/resource/resource25_core.py:117 has
     Q(H)=997*25*d/15000+eps*(Hcap^2-H^2).
   On its acquired phase interval this is at least997*25*d/15000. At actual
   m,15000*Q(H)/(997*m) bounds physical D, so it transfers as well. Its
   artificial positive curvature is not an additional physical assertion.

Interfaces that must change or be reacquired
-------------------------------------------
A. Acquire every initial/intermediate physical signed support radius at the
   actual m before using the dependent moment improvement. Gaussian-line
   support arithmetic is fixed-index. The initial and contracted support
   calls in Q/initial_q2.py:50 and Q/resource/resource25_core.py:79-96,135-144
   cannot simply be relabeled. A stronger sharp predecessor supplies q=0;
   the Q2 stored q=25*(T(rho_lo)-CE_lower) does not by itself transfer as the
   universal same-law allowance at larger m, where the multiplier is m.
   A valid actual-index owncap is also needed. The universal T(rho_lo) cap
   used in Q2 is independent of m and is a convenient sufficient premise.
   Any other n25 cap derived from a predecessor smoothing formula requires
   actual-index checking. Support rho/beta bounds must come from the same
   uniformly established incoming law state, not from a later output.

B. With physical lambda_m=m*s, transfer a nonnegative affine resource as
     D_actual >= b + g*inward + (25/m)*h25*lambda_m.
   The old joint caps d0,Lambda remain conservative by mD<=25d and b,g>=0.
   Set the new count's n=m,D=(25/m)*d,noise_charge=(25/m)*h25; retain b,g and
   use phase normalization15000/(997*m). Changing only n is unsound.
   Preserve optional moment/sign-premise fields in any generalized count
   contract as physical same-law bounds.

C. The stored harmonic lambda geometry cap is not uniform. Its only valid
   reuse in the acquisition route is the theta bound:
     theta_m <=1/[1+m*vmin/Lambda+vmin/(B*d0)] <=theta_25.
   Do not import lambda_geometry_cap25 as a bound on lambda_m.

D. Preserve the finite selected acquisition sequence even if rerunning the
   adaptive proposer would choose other radii or a different stopping time.
   Existing fixed-n receipts, index gates and source hashes remain intact.
   A new named uniform composition must bind the exact floor25 DAG, this
   source extension, every actual-index support proof, and the corrected
   count interfaces. Then complete exact count forests at each intended
   actual index. A branch entirely excluded by INITIAL/resources needs no
   count forest, but still needs the source and support composition.

Conclusion of this review
-------------------------
The Cantelli and product-gap additions themselves are compatible with the
floor25 mechanism. The outstanding obligations are exact floor25 arithmetic
for new feedback branches, actual-index support/contact/owncap acquisition,
a separately named fully bound interpretation, and actual-index counts
where a branch survives. This is a conditional route, not a finished
uniform theorem or a completed cutoff25 proof.

Exact beta0 source-family addendum and conditional actual26..30 composition

This extends the source mapping in UNIFORM25_CANTELLI_PRODUCTGAP_REVIEW.txt

New exact source mapping
------------------------
The beta0 ordinary INITIAL equals the previously reviewed product-gap
INITIAL plus only the two p-local mean-cap lines. Removing those lines
restores quarter3_resource_feedback_probe/initial34_product_gap.py exactly.
Its three caps are square roots of
  p_upper*(1+beta_upper)/2,
  (1-p_lower)*(1-beta_lower)/2,
  max_{p in the fixed interval} p*(1-p).
These are physical Cauchy and variance inequalities without any n. Their
fixed p/beta cell bounds are therefore valid in the same uniform induction.
The indexed panel-export loop is algebraically identical to the original
closed-panel loop. Exact common-HIGH and integer arithmetic alter neither
the bounds nor the index dependence; the fixed upward-M hypothesis and
Cantelli candidates are already covered by the floor25 review.

The candidate/resource contracted arithmetic is byte-identical to the
product-gap family in that review; the exact wrapper port has a separate
bound review. The new resource wrapper merely fixes this profile and permits
residual panels22,23. Local INITIAL imports the same frozen INITIAL runtime
and uses physical bounds already acquired by that resource sequence.

Uniform finite induction
------------------------
Keep raw_actual<=raw_saved and m*D_actual<=25*d_saved at every charged
step. In physical moment, mean, endpoint and support deductions, the weaker
D_actual<=d_saved remains available. Global fixed export relaxation, outward
guard rounding, and minima of valid raw/D upper bounds preserve the weighted
invariant. The resource's saved raw/D outputs have the same interpretation
by the previously reviewed floor25 charged phase formulas.

Local p/rho/beta/gamma/mean intersections retain properties of that same
unrestricted attained witness. They do not invoke a constrained maximizer.
The positive-gap and raw<10 guards are conservative at actual m>=25. The
local first and second covers use the unchanged floor25 Fourier/HIGH
coefficients; their terminal complete empty covers therefore exclude the
same possible physical witnesses at m. The p-local mean additions create
no new n-dependent term. No actual lambda_m cap is imported from the old
harmonic geometry cap; only its already-reviewed theta consequence is used.

All support states must be reacquired in their original dependency order
at the actual m. The structural inventory contains two global INITIAL and
ten resource support objects, and no additional local support object. The
two local profiles reuse acquired physical radii; each such radius already
comes from the preceding resource's fresh actual-index support check.
Universal T(7/4)=3619319/8750000 is an index-free actual own cap. A sharp
actual predecessor supplies q=0; the positive old n25 universal allowance
is not transplanted to a larger index.

Bounded composition review
--------------------------
Reviewed shared/bridge25_high_beta0/compose.py and PROOF.txt. Independently

A16. INITIAL31_UNIFORM_INTERPRETATION.md
Original ordinary proof: work/n25-mixed-current28-source-v1/n25-first28-source-cache/cutoff25_20261011/continuation_20261010/audit35/INITIAL31_UNIFORM_INTERPRETATION.md

============================================================================

# Conditional uniform interpretation of the exact INITIAL31 enclosure

This is a mathematical reuse route, not a claim that the fixed-index receipt already proves a bridge. The original artifacts are unchanged.

## Proposed statement

Let N=31. Assume an attained positive closed maximizing law at an actual integer m>=N satisfies C_m>=CE, the same rho branch and support radii as a selected INITIAL31 branch, and the branch's declared score cap (at most .432313). Subject to the retained analytic enclosure formulas, the eight-pass INITIAL31 computation can be interpreted as a **uniform floor-N enclosure** for that law.

The needed invariant after a pass is stronger than a plain unweighted defect bound:

    raw remainder <= raw_stored,
    m * D_actual <= N * d_stored.

Consequently D_actual<=d_stored. A later computation that exports a noise allowance proportional to N*d must use the weighted invariant, not multiply an arbitrary unweighted bound by N.

## Source map and argument

Main source: `threshold31_20261008/root/exact_initial31/initial_local33_charged.py` (I below).

1. `exact_initial_branches.py`:18–21 fixes n=nmax=31 and K=0. I:34 uses nmax only for c0=.4095+K/nmax. Thus the score lower endpoint is independent of the actual index in this sharp case.
2. Moment feasibility, sign-probability covers, support-deficit refinements, gap endpoint bounds, and moment cells in I:45–92 and I:104–136 do not use the index. They remain legitimate enclosures for the same rho branch and support. The coarse inequality D_actual<=d_stored is sufficient for their geometry.
3. I:93–103 uses low/high Fourier bounds evaluated at N. In `kernel.py`:79–83, a0=(N-1)/(2N) and b0=(45-M)(1-2/N)/96 increase with the actual index; their inverse powers and the root constant decrease. All additive constants are nonnegative. The extra L2/sqrt(N) also decreases. Therefore the floor-N triple remains valid.
4. `high_window_core.py`:17–18 checks 2*N*c>1 for every exponential term; lines 23–31 check the additional monotonicity gates. The six-term HIGH expression is explicitly designed for all indices above its input floor. Choosing the best complete window at N gives one valid uniform bound; one does not need the identity of the minimizing window to remain the same later.
5. I:137 uses phi_lo=.3988*(1-2.5*raw/N). Under the raw invariant, the actual lower density bound at m is at least this. Keeping the larger fixed interval [phi_lo,.4] is conservative. The same observation licenses I:150's extra phase curvature at N. Its coefficient .4095*rho_lo-d/15 is positive by a checked gate.
6. I:140–143 uses an own-score numerator raw/N - .3988*(1-2.5*raw/N)*D_lower/6. For fixed nonnegative raw,D_lower this increases with 1/N. It therefore bounds the actual numerator at m>=N from above; a negative floor-N numerator legitimately excludes a law at any such m.
7. I:151 subtracts max(K,N*(c-.40974))*rho_lo. With K=0, actual m*(c-CE)*rho is at least max(0,N*(c-.40974))*rho_lo. The floor subtraction is valid. The breakpoint c=.40974 is explicitly included in I:145. On each side the subtraction is affine, preserving the score-corner convexity argument.
8. I:153 multiplies the largest budget by 15000/(997*N). The underlying bound is m*D_actual <= (15000/997)*budget. Thus the stored value proves m*D_actual<=N*d_stored, exactly the required weighted invariant. Taking minima with the previous defect in I:155 preserves that invariant, as does taking maxima across every contributing moment cell in I:163.
9. The raw and phase outputs also remain valid at the floor because their coefficient enclosures were proved pointwise for all actual m>=N. No claim is needed that the rounded output of rerunning the program at m would be numerically monotone.

The induction uses the same fixed moment-cell construction with the stored invariants; it does not rely on comparing independently rerun adaptive covers at different indices.

## What must still be proved before using this as a bridge

* Acquire the branch support radii for each intended index. `direct_gaussian_support.py`:58–74 performs a Gaussian-line calculation at a fixed index. No all-index monotonicity of that entire line calculation has been proved in this note. A support check at 31 is not by itself a support check at 32–34.
* Supply the own-score cap independently at the intended index, and acquire the correct contact/predecessor allowance for the support calculation. The proposed q<=.31 premise is not consumed by INITIAL itself, but is relevant to acquiring support.
* Trace the downstream resource refinement from INITIAL to the count contract. This note covers the INITIAL enclosure, not every later branch-specific acquisition callback or changed support radius.
* Create a separately named interpretation/interface artifact binding the exact INITIAL source and proof hashes, recording the weighted invariant and intended index range. Do not relabel `SHARP31_BRANCH_INITIAL_V1` or edit its profile into an apparent historical replay.
* Complete an exact count proof at each bridge index. A floating count or resource discovery record remains discovery evidence only.

Potential value: if the subsequent resource refinement also has a verified uniform floor-N interpretation, support acquisition and bridge counts may suffice without repeating every INITIAL arithmetic pass. Potential failure point: silently treating stored `D` as an ordinary unweighted bound while importing a lambda allowance that needs mD<=ND would invalidate the reuse.
