A17. CURRENT4042-CENTRAL-FLOOR40-APPLICABILITY-v1.md
Original ordinary proof: work/CURRENT4042-CENTRAL-FLOOR40-APPLICABILITY-v1.md
Use in main: C40--42: eight exact floor40 applicability implications and signed repair
============================================================================

# Conditional floor40 interpretation for the literal indices 40, 41 and 42

This is a new source-level mathematical implication. It creates no numerical
acceptance, current support claim or all-law theorem. In particular, the original
`SINGLE40` objects, their `N=40` fields, all execution records and the two source
interfaces keep their original bytes and meaning. A future assembler must attach
this new implication explicitly; it cannot rename `SINGLE40` as an actual41 run.

## Exact owner and quantities

Fix one actual index `m in {40,41,42}` and assume a strict failure `C_m>C_E`.
Extract and orient ONE unrestricted global positive closed-CDF owner X at m,
then keep X, its raw sum threshold H, its sign probability p, signed moments,
and conditional noises throughout every acquisition and comparison. Owners at
different indices may differ. No optimizer is chosen separately inside a p or
rho cell. Let `rho=E|X|^3`, `gamma=EX^3`, `beta=EX|X|` and let theta be the
original total sign-conditional variance. The noise coordinate is the ACTUAL
`lambda=m*p*(1-p)*theta/(1-theta)`.

Use exactly the physical defect and raw remainder in manuscript2b5:
`D=(3+sqrt10)*rho-gamma-3J >=0`, with J the full gap functional, and raw
remainder R from equation `rescore:re-raw`. Do not replace D by one of its lower
bounds, confuse R with a scalar final score, or equate lambda with theta.
The tables' T/raw values bound R; the tables' d/D values have the WEIGHTED
meaning `m*D_actual <= 40*d`. The original inward coordinate, joint intercepts
and every signed phase corner keep their definitions.

The incoming global statements are genuine weak C39 and weak C38. Inductively,
the current weak predecessor is available when treating41 and42. Therefore the
SAME-owner decrement is0, hence satisfies the older loose delta40<=.19 premise
and the indexed zero-decrement41/42 premise. Deleted one/two-summand score caps
are below.4253 from these global statements, without a tail43 or sharp40 premise.

The earlier rational scalar enclosure remains a conservative common witness:

```
CElo=819/2000, CEhi=40974/100000,
r=10131/10000, sqrt(m)<13/2, sqrt(m-1)>6, m-1>=38,
e=(13/2)/(15*38*6)+(2/25)*(13/2)/38^2=1469/649800,
U=r*(CEhi+19/4000)+e=1371664491931/3249000000000 < .4226,
42*(U-CElo)=288392943517/541500000000 < .54.
```

Actual weak predecessors are stronger than the deliberately loose `CE+19/4000`
used here. Thus the required owner cap.4226, the comparison cap.4247 and
`0<m*(C_m-C_E)<.54` hold without assuming the desired C_m bound. The first
positive39 comparison is needed inside the upstream proof of sharp39, not as a
replacement for the genuine incoming sharp39 theorem in this consumer.

## The conditional extension theorem

Suppose the complete current arithmetic predicates for all ten original62C
components are supplied (the old eight completed children plus the two genuine
interface recoveries), with exact source/candidate/parent bytes and all controls.
Also require the separate whole two-pass indexed INITIAL-support predicate
`fifth_heat.target(40,42,K0,R=3.84,q0)`. Assume the retained source contact,
Fourier/moment, Sperner and local comparison lemmas, with the exact gates below.
Then the same conditional CENTRAL tables give their complete physical resources
for each actual m=40,41,42. LEFT retains its separately indexed40--42 meaning.

This theorem does NOT follow from the indexed INITIAL-support predicate alone.
Every later support implication is justified below. Without acceptance of these
new floor implications, the old `SINGLE40` arithmetic predicate supplies only40.

## 1. Initialization, moment feedback and signed phase

`root/initial/accept_initial40.py` uses floor40/nmax48/K0. The only numerical
score-floor use of nmax is K/nmax=0. The actual first-stage domain and all
signed cells are retained. No initial raw or defect invariant is supplied by an
archived success bit. The source raw feedback induction uses R, and the physical
defect feedback uses `mD<=40d` at EVERY update.

The raw identity, Gaussian weight removal and conserved phase price are in
manuscript lines1580--1600,1662--1675,2380--2430,3330--3440 and3850--3895.
For `m>=40`, a prior raw ceiling r<16 gives the density interval
`phi(H/sqrt(m)) in [phi0_lower*(1-5*r/80), .4]`.
This uses the original nonnegative phase term; it is not an unsupported bound
`H^2<=5r`. A new upper raw budget B gives `mD<=B/kappa` and hence the new
floor table entry `d=B/(40*kappa)`, kappa=997/15000. Negative B excludes the
law before dividing. An unweighted bound D<=d is never promoted to mD<=40d.

All nonnegative LOW/HIGH errors are evaluated at40 to bound their analytic
values for m>=40. The source gates include the domain for every positive
`sqrt(m)*exp(-c*m)` term and the permitted improved Mills branch. The factors
`(m/(m-1))^(3/2)`, its positive products with1/(m-1), and positive inverse
powers of the LOW denominators decrease. Monotonicity is asserted for the real
analytic envelopes; the rounded exponential implementation need not be monotone.
Every moment rectangle, actual-support radius, HIGH event and optional paired
eligibility gate is retained, including R>2*gamma_upper where required.

For the general phase curvature, set g0=3.16*rho-3-d and t=(d-.16)_+.
Its derivative in the density endpoint is `(g0+t)/2>=0`: for d<=.16 use
rho>=1 and g0>=.16-d; for d>=.16 the sum is3.16*(rho-1).
Thus the floor lower-density curvature is a valid lower price. The corrected
paired expression is instead
`C*rho+min(phi_lower*g0,.4*g0)/2`.
It treats a negative g0 at the UPPER density endpoint, so no signed term is
silently declared monotone. Linear coefficients use both density endpoints.
The conserved extra curvature has nonnegative checked coefficient
`(.4095*rho-d/15)/2` and the weaker floor density factor. All four coherent
corners and both H signs are retained. Their complete envelopes and H roots
therefore bound the same actual law at m, without an actual41/42 kernel call.

The moment/gap updates between Fourier steps have no additional actual index.
Their dependence on n is solely the licensed weighted defect update and the
noise conversion above. Multiplying a physical lower inequality by m and using
m>=40 gives exactly the floor joint intercepts. In particular, on lambda in
[a,b], retain simultaneously
`theta<=b/(40*vmin+b)` and `d_in<=d0*(1-a/Lambda)` whenever used. The code's
original caps, supports, signed moments and compatibility constraints apply
to ONE law, not to independently spendable budgets.

## 2. Every CENTRAL Gaussian support contraction

The actual CENTRAL support calls are `acquisition_core.radius_gates` and
`strip_gates`, both using `panel_exterior40.support_cell`. Later confinement
and profile calls use Sperner, not `fifth_heat.target`.

For any one complete x/H cell, write the exact upper bound of support_cell at
an actual m as

```
B_m = LP_upper + .54*Q_positive/m
      +.4*(M1/m+M2/m^2+M3/m^3+M4/m^4+M5/m^5+Tail/m^6)
      +rho*(sqrt(m/(m-1))*delta_m+.384*.4226/m).
```

Every Mj is nonnegative: the first three explicitly take max(0,Bernstein
upper); the last two are absolute monomial sums. Tail is positive. Q_positive
is max(0,Bernstein upper). LP_upper is independent of m and may be negative.
All moment, radius and signed-beta arguments are already acquired uniform
bounds of the same law. Since delta_m<=.19, every m-dependent positive term
is bounded by its m=40/.19 value. Therefore `B_m<=B_40<0` on the WHOLE closed
cell. This holds for both rays and for all cells of the transformed strip;
the original prior-support interval closes the external remainder. The finite
Taylor remainder uses the same radius and H bounds and improves for m>=40.

This argument explicitly discards favorable Gaussian coefficients. It would
NOT justify reusing a fifth-heat target which retained its favorable fifth
term: that theorem needs upper endpoint42. This is why the indexed INITIAL
primitive is separate. LEFT's `acquisition_core.support` already records the
single40/.19 AND finite40--42/q0 proofs at every selected heat radius; neither
those proofs nor the CENTRAL monotone Gaussian proof can be replaced by a
renamed old support record.

## 3. Sperner confinement and noise profiles

Keep the entire source gates: p away from0 and1/2, theta<1, positive gaps,
support/buffer/disjointness, positive curvatures, both signs and a complete
closed H/noise grid. The chosen fd is strictly above
`sqrt(max((40/39)^3,40/38))`; it bounds both actual deletion factors at every
m>=40. The binomial-mode interval at J=38 contains the actual interval at
J=m-2, and its variance and Stirling factors are weaker. No mode is omitted.

Choose the source floor L and U as valid density LOWER and UPPER bounds; do
not plug a larger actual lower density into an error term that needs an upper
coefficient. Actual `phi_lower(m)>=phi_lower(40)`. Every subtracted derivative
error uses a nonnegative numerator divided by m, so the floor lower bound
remains valid; every added error similarly gives a valid floor upper bound.
For the lower-ray argument the SAME chosen lower derivative is integrated on
both intervals, with the checked positive width. The upper-ray allocation uses
the checked positive `dc=L+U`; the lower uses checked positive L.

Let raw>=0 be the common nonnegative error/moment budget BEFORE the binary
penalty, P>=0 that penalty, z>=0 and Dist>=0. Feasibility implies raw-mP>=0.
The ordinary error is bounded by
`(z*raw+Dist)/m-z*P+Bad*bad_m`.
Only its NONNEGATIVE inverse numerator is divided using m>=40; the negative
term -zP is retained unchanged. It is invalid to drop or independently spend
this common penalty. The source empty-cell test raw-40P<=0 is safe for every m.

The remaining bad-pair probability uses nonnegative d and tau>0. Exactly
`g_m(d)=d/(m*tau+d)<=g_40(d)` for m>=40. The source derivative and intercept
`40*tau/(40*tau+s)^2`, `s^2/(40*tau+s)^2` form a global upper tangent of the
concave g_40 at s>=0, including s=0. Thus the original finite tangent choices
bound g_m without claiming their actual-m tangent coefficients are equal.
The complete moment/noise feasible triangle lies inside the weaker triangle
formed with raw-40P and40; its vertex coefficients and Bad are nonnegative.
The independently valid Cantelli noise fraction also weakens to floor40.
Taking the minimum of these complete valid upper bounds is legitimate.

Consequently every source strict Sperner margin excludes the same signed
exterior for actual m. The resulting amplitude bound becomes an X-support
radius by the original p/ell transformation, which has NO n dependence.
Every subsequent feedback call again uses the preceding weighted invariant,
actual support and whole moment field set. The profile's joint-noise restriction
uses the ACTUAL lambda cell. Inherited amplitudes are inherited only with their
complete source dominance check. This covers all later CENTRAL contractions.

## 4. Final TINY, MID and LOW comparisons

The CENTRAL normalization tables use the integer floor40. Their count/entropy
segment at p+-delta/40 contains the actual p+-delta/m segment. All positive
log-factorial, entropy, variance and ratio errors are therefore bounded by the
same source constants, with all source denominator/domain gates retained.
Mass lower bounds improve; the proved normalized mass upper bound1 remains.

For each signed Gaussian line, the positive-phase lower derivative is
`1-A_m/m`, with `0<=A_m<=A_40`, and the negative-phase upper derivative is
`1/(1-eta_m/m)`, with `0<=eta_m<=eta_40<40`.
The exact Hplus/Hminus and phase intervals, variance gate40*v>delta^2, and the
entire source line domain remain. These inequalities supply the same fixed
L/U bounds at every m>=40; no favorable signed coefficient is replaced blindly.
The cubic repair is kept nonnegative, or a signed cubic gain is minimized over
its whole positive multiplier interval before it enters a repair ceiling.
TINY's strict reserve and MID's full signed reserves then remain valid with
their original companions, rather than assuming a discrete N40 label is a law.

The LOW reduction has the separate essential finite-band treatment: if I is the
retained upper inverse correction (of EITHER sign), then
`I/m <= max(I/40,I/42)` for40<=m<=42. The source code implements EXACTLY this
maximum. It also uses `max(mass_floor*bracket,bracket)`, covering either sign
of bracket under the entire mass interval[mass_floor,1]. No negative I is
replaced by I/40 alone. The current full304 row processes and two original
binders must verify this exact code and its complete contexts. The binomial
gap is the minimum of the three actual40/41/42 per-panel gaps, not a general
Bernoulli zero-noise bound substituted for the necessary positive gap.

The complete local union is TINY at0<lambda<=.05; MID on.05--.08 at all
phases; on.08--.10 MID covers absolute phase.19--.5 and LOW its complement;
on.10--.12 the seam is.27; above.12 LOW covers the full phase. Source smaller

Every selected profile/noise subinterval is intersected with the SAME actual

# Active CENTRAL40 overrides: explicit source/math addendum

This addendum preserves `CURRENT4042-CENTRAL-FLOOR40-APPLICABILITY-v1.md`
(`d81e459c…`) and its JSON source ledger (`03b8710b…`). It corrects the
**completeness of its source anchors**, not the labels or bytes of SINGLE40.
It supplies no numerical completion, all-law theorem, or automatic acceptance.

## Actual import and installation order

In the source-qualified `acquisition4042-v1/source-final/payload/.../central40`
namespace, `candidate_panel40.py` imports `contracted_kernel as k` and executes:

1. `high_window_core.install(k)`: replaces `k.high` with `install.best`, which
   takes the minimum of the entire six-piece `install.one` over seven explicit
   allowed windows (the passed window plus six fixed alternatives).
2. `paired_extended.install(k)`: replaces `k.paired` with `install.paired` and
   `k.paired_eligible` with `install.eligible`.
3. `signed_variance_resource.install(k)`: captures the then-current
   `k.general_cell` and replaces it by `install.general_cell`. This wrapper
   calls that original function first and preserves its result on the
   non-applicable signed-probability domain.

Then `acquisition_core.py` imports `candidate_panel40 as c` and executes
`corrected_phase_callback_extended.install(c.k)`: this captures the original
`contracted_kernel.late_envelope` and replaces it with the sign-corrected
`install.corrected` callback. Original `late_envelope` resolves the **installed**
`paired` and `high` through its module globals at call time. Thus neither the
base `paired` nor the base `high` is the final active implementation here.

`high_window_core.py` also imports the ordinary `kernel` to provide its default
argument. The actual installation explicitly passes `contracted_kernel`, so
that default is not used as the target. `paired_conditional_gamma47.py` is
retained in the exact original acquisition/confinement/profile manifests, but
is **not imported or installed in this selected CENTRAL branch**. Its older
`M<=2, g<=1` bounds must not replace the actual extended bounds below.

The accompanying machine-readable addendum records whole bytes and nested AST
function spans for all eight relevant modules. It also checks the exact four
installation statements; no module is executed by that source census.

## HIGH_WINDOW: every active term has the required floor direction

Fix the same law/moment/event parameters, an allowed window w and the source
floor N=40. The active gates retain 0<a<=1, m_event>0, 0<d0<=12,
1<=M<=111/10, 0<w<=1/4, positive sine slope h, and
`2*N*c>1` for each of the four listed damping constants. None may be dropped.

For actual integer m>=N, the six analytical terms evaluated at N remain upper
bounds: the first, third and fifth have factors sqrt(m)*exp(-m*c) with
2*N*c>1; the second is a nonnegative constant plus a nonnegative constant/m;
the base fourth term is exp(-m*k*b^2)/sqrt(m); and the comparator is
sqrt(m)*exp(-m/8)*(2*r(m/8)+g_c/3), where
`r(x)=(x+2)/(x^2+4*x+2)` is positive and decreasing and N>=5.

The active optional first-piece improvement also retains positive `slope`,
`cquarter>0` and `2*N*cquarter>1`; its extra factor r(m*slope/4) decreases.
The optional zero-piece improvement is used only when x=N*k*b^2>=1/4.
For f(x)=sqrt(x)*exp(-x)*r(x), the logarithmic derivative has positive
denominator `2*x*(x+2)*(x^2+4*x+2)` and numerator

`4-10*x-22*x^2-13*x^3-2*x^4`.

At x=1/4 this is negative, and it strictly decreases for positive x. Hence
this improvement also decreases for every actual m>=N. These are bounds for
the exact analytical expressions: no monotonicity assertion about rounded
`exphi` implementations is needed. Each floor evaluation already rounds
outward. The minimum of valid window bounds remains a valid bound.

## Extended paired LOW: actual active domain and floor factors

The actual domain is `0<p_l<p_u<1/2`, `1<=M<3`, `g>=0`, `g^2<2`,
`RADIUS>2*g`, and `M-1-gamma_lower^2>=0`. Eligibility also keeps
`0<=theta<1`. The conditional lower rho and signed-third-moment formulas,
the fifth-moment rectangle, Mills-cancellation norm bounds and all their
positive-divisor/nonnegative-coefficient gates remain exactly the source ones.
They are same-law moment statements, independent of the actual sample index.

The active paired return uses

- `fac=(m/(m-1))^(3/2)`, decreasing for m>1;
- positive additional coefficients proportional to `fac/(m-1)`;
- `a0=(m-1)/(2*m)` and **`b0=(45-M)/96*(1-2/m)`**, both positive and increasing;
- only nonnegative numerators in the remainder
  `e/(2*a0^2)+B^2/(2*b0^3)`.

Consequently `lc`, `lh`, `l2` and `l2/sqrt(m)` are bounded above by the
corresponding N=40 expressions, under the same retained domain. The actual
extended `b0` must not be replaced by the inactive base `43/96` expression.
Bounds on physical rho, gamma, moments and support are never acquired merely
by eligibility or by this index comparison.

## Sign-corrected curvature is the active phase callback

`corrected` first calls the original four-corner envelope and verifies every
returned tuple has all five fields. With `phi_low=PHI*(1-5*raw/(2*m))`, it
replaces each original curvature by

`Q=C*rho + min(phi_low*g0, (2/5)*g0)/2`,
`g0=(79/25)*rho-3-d`.

This is the lower curvature for the full normal-density interval
`phi in [phi_low,2/5]`, including negative g0. Under fixed raw bound and
positive phi_low, increasing m cannot reduce this lower curvature: if g0>=0
it increases with phi_low; if g0<0 it is the constant upper-density endpoint.
The retained source checks require `Q>0` and `Q<=Q_original` for each corner.
The four complete (C,rho) corners, both signed linear endpoints and the
conserved-phase positive addition stay present. No proof may insert a larger
unverified curvature or drop the negative-g0 branch.

For the active general envelope the curvature has the additional term
`-(2/5-phi_low)*max(d-4/25,0)/2`. Its derivative in phi_low is
`((79/25)*rho-3-d+max(d-4/25,0))/2>=0` for rho>=1, by separately checking
d<=4/25 and d>=4/25. This is the exact signed correction used in V1.

## Signed variance wrapper is not an extra sample-index estimate

The installed wrapper retains the whole original general-cell computation,
then bounds sign-specific third/fourth/fifth moments and the outer gap by
nonnegative residual weighted-defect allowances with positive `kp,km`.
Its operations contain no sample-index variable. The same-law moment and
support domain, physical defect bound, signed p interval, root directions,
nonnegative residual and final moment/linear rectangle gates are therefore
the premises for every actual m. They are carried by the floor40 INITIAL and
weighted `m*D_actual<=40*d` invariant; they are not inferred from the literal
name SINGLE40. Root rounding is upward for upper bounds and downward where a
lower bound or curvature is required, as preserved by the original code.

The finite m=40,41,42 applicability conclusion of V1 is conditional on these
**active** analytic bounds, its other source domains and full current numeric
predicates. This addendum closes the omitted override-anchor issue; it does
not manufacture actual41/42 numerical runs or an analytical licence.
